## 32. GEX call-wall-distance — CLOSED: REJECTED at Stage 1 (2026-09-22)

**V1 is closed (Section 18). Chosen next direction: GEX/gamma exposure, specifically the call-wall-distance hypothesis** — infrastructure already exists and is validated (`gex_data_loader.py`→`gex_calculator.py`→`gex_lookup.py`), the prospective pipeline is running again after the 2026-09-20 timezone fix, and `gex_lookup.py`'s own code confirms the regime signal is testable (Section 18's resolved contradiction).

**Explicit goal this time: pre-registered BEFORE looking at results, not backfilled after (unlike Phase 6/7, which self-label as non-preregistered — Section 18).**

**Draft pre-registration: `preregister_gex_callwall_experiment.py`** — constructs a real `ExperimentConfig` (the same class ML Experiment 1 used), modeled directly on `preregister_experiment1.py`'s structure. **Deliberately contains no path that can register or execute anything** — no call to `preregister_experiment()` or `run_experiment()` anywhere in the file. Ran it: constructs cleanly against the real, unmodified validator (no `InvalidExperimentConfigError`), producing a genuine computed `config_hash` (`9820ed6e18c11f7fa069a931490823398081e469ee5c01c95cec7009b3745fde`) — proof this exact specification, not a paraphrase, is what would be locked if and when it's actually submitted.

**Signal definition** (verified by reading `gex_lookup.py` in full, not from memory): `call_wall_distance_pct = (call_wall_strike − spot_price) / spot_price × 100`, from the T-1 validated GEX snapshot, `sign_convention="model_a"`, enforced no-lookahead by an executable assertion in the lookup function itself.

**Hypothesis (directional, not just "nonzero correlation"):** among V1-qualifying call signals, signals closer to a call wall show systematically *suppressed* forward returns versus signals farther from any wall — the standard GEX-literature dealer-hedging-resistance theory, applied here for the first time as a specific, falsifiable prediction rather than an unexamined intuition.

**Two-stage design, specifically to avoid Phase 6/7's methodology gap:**
- **Stage 1 (historical, available now):** the existing 2026-06-10 to 2026-09-08 GEX-covered window (748 snapshot rows, `gex_data_cache.db:gex_metrics`, confirmed by direct query). Locked and hashed *before* the correlation is computed.
- **Stage 2 (prospective, true out-of-sample):** signals logged by `prospective_gex_pipeline.py` strictly *after* this config's eventual preregistration timestamp — genuinely never-seen data at design time, not just a held-out slice of already-collected data.

**Pre-specified decision rules (fixed now, before any result exists):** VALIDATED requires joined Stage-1 N≥100, primary 20-bar test significant at Bonferroni-corrected α=0.0125 (4 horizons: 5/10/20/50-bar), effect size ≥0.5pp, survival of all 5 declared robustness checks (leave-one-ticker-out, 3 alternate horizons, both sign conventions), **and** Stage-2 replication in the same direction. REJECTED if Stage 1 runs (N≥100) but fails significance/effect-size/robustness. INCONCLUSIVE if N<100, or Stage 1 passes but Stage 2 can't run yet, or the two stages disagree with no pre-specified tiebreaker.

**Honest data-sufficiency assessment, as explicitly requested — the test was NOT run, only this assessment:**
- **Stage 1 (historical) is plausibly, but not confirmedly, ready.** 748 GEX-snapshot rows exist (62-64 days/ticker), comparable in scale to Phase 6's 60-day/579-signal window — but the actual *joined* N (V1-qualifying signals that also have GEX available at T-1) has deliberately **not been computed** in this pass, since doing so would start the analysis before the config is really locked. Informed guess only: plausibly in the same order of magnitude as Phase 6's 579, likely clearing the N≥100 threshold — but this is an estimate, not a count, and should be treated as such until the join actually runs.
- **Stage 2 (prospective) is clearly NOT ready.** Current n=11, two orders of magnitude below the N≥100 threshold, uneven ticker coverage (2 of 6 tickers with zero rows), and the 2026-09-20 fix hasn't even been empirically confirmed to produce new rows yet (market closed since the fix; first real test is Monday 2026-09-22's run). At recent accumulation rates, reaching n=100 could plausibly take months, not weeks — consistent with this project's own "weeks/months is fine" philosophy (Section 1), but a genuinely long wait, not a formality.
- **Recommendation:** Stage 1 could reasonably be run soon (to at least establish the real joined N and confirm testability), but genuine VALIDATED status requires Stage 2, which needs sustained prospective collection over an extended period. Do not shortcut Stage 2 by treating Stage 1 alone as sufficient — that would just be a more careful version of what Phase 6/7 already did, not the improvement this pre-registration is meant to be.

**Nothing has been submitted to the real framework and no test has run.** Next deliberate step, only when approved: actually call `preregister_experiment(config)` from this file (or a copy of it) to lock the config into `research_database.db`.

**Status 2026-09-21, per explicit user decision: wait-and-monitor phase, not active-work phase.** Agreed not to shortcut Stage 2 — genuinely wait for prospective data to accumulate before running anything. **In-progress monitoring task, not yet complete:** confirm the 2026-09-20 timezone fix (Section 7 item 4) actually holds over real trading days, not just that it compiled cleanly. **If you're picking this up in a new session and no confirmation has been reported yet:** query `prospective_gex_validation.db`'s `raw_signals` table directly (`COUNT(*)`, `MAX(trading_date)`, per-ticker breakdown) and check `gex_collection_log.txt`'s most recent entries for a sensible (non-stuck) date, exactly like the original fix diagnosis did. Pre-fix baseline for comparison: 11 rows, `MAX(trading_date)=2026-09-09`, AAPL=4/AMD=5/MSTR=1/TSLA=1/NVDA=0/SMCI=0. Confirmation requires 3 real trading days behaving sensibly (date advancing correctly, whether or not a new signal row was logged that specific day); report a regression instead if 5 trading days pass and the date still looks stuck. A session-local `CronCreate` job (id `4c044f75`, weekdays 8:12am ET, auto-expires after 7 days or session end) was set up to check this proactively, but it only survives if that session stays open — this note is the reliable fallback if it doesn't.

**Stage 1 dataset definition update, 2026-09-22 — documentation only, no result computed yet.** Section 40's backfill grew `gex_metrics` from 748 to 850 rows (2026-06-10 to 2026-09-21, was 2026-06-10 to 2026-09-08), and the new `GEX_Historical_Incremental_Update` task keeps it growing daily going forward. This changes what "Stage 1" concretely means for this experiment, and the reasoning for why that's acceptable is recorded here explicitly, before any Stage 1 number is computed, not discovered after the fact:

- **No result was ever computed against the original 748-row set.** Stage 1's correlation/N has not been run for this experiment (Section 32 above states this plainly — "the test was NOT run, only this assessment"). There is nothing to reshuffle after a peek; this is a pre-registration-time dataset-definition decision, not a post-hoc do-over.
- **No lookahead is introduced.** The newly-added dates (2026-09-09 through 2026-09-20) are calendar-historical — real market data that already existed and was already fixed/immutable before this pre-registration was drafted (2026-09-21) and before its `config_hash` was computed. The backfill only computed GEX *metrics* for those already-past dates into the cache table; it did not create new market data or shift any date boundary forward in time relative to "now." The distinction that matters for lookahead is *when the underlying raw data existed*, not *when it was computed into this specific table* — and the underlying data existed well before pre-registration.
- **Stage 1's dataset is hereby defined as the full historical `gex_metrics` table, not a fixed 748-row snapshot.** As of 2026-09-22 that's 850 rows spanning 2026-06-10 to 2026-09-21, and it will keep growing daily via the new incremental job (Section 40) for as long as that job runs. Future re-reads of this experiment should use whatever the table currently contains as of whenever Stage 1 is actually executed, not freeze at 748 or 850.
- **This is a deliberate, reasoned call, not an oversight.** Flagging it explicitly here so a future session doesn't mistake the row-count change for a data-integrity problem or a methodology drift — it's a natural consequence of Section 40's approved backfill, reasoned through before Stage 1 was ever run.

**Still not run.** This section documents the dataset-definition reasoning only — Stage 1's actual N and correlation for the GEX call-wall-distance hypothesis remain uncomputed, exactly as before this note.

### Stage 1 EXECUTED, 2026-09-22 — REJECTED. Experiment CLOSED, no Stage 2.

**Config updated before running, per the dataset-definition reasoning above**: `date_range`/`train_period` → `("2026-06-10", "2026-09-21")` (the table's actual extent at run time), `current_historical_gex_snapshot_rows` → 850. New `config_hash`: `3f0e6b6523f66efa1469ba590d7b56750051defaf1fd474cc515981a55f0d6cf` (supersedes `9820ed6e18c11f7fa069a931490823398081e469ee5c01c95cec7009b3745fde`).

**Signal generation reused Phase 6's own already-validated V1-qualifying-signal methodology unchanged** (`phase6_primary_control.py`'s bullish-regime + 20-bar-breakout + entry-score≥20 construction), extended over the widened window, joined against `gex_lookup.get_gex_for_signal()` at T-1.

**Joined N = 772** (795 V1-qualifying signals → 775 with all 4 horizon returns computable → 772 with GEX available at T-1; 3 excluded for missing GEX/wall data). 68 distinct calendar days. **Clears min_n_per_stage=100.**

**Test construction — disclosed explicitly since the pre-registration named the test family ("day-clustered Monte Carlo, matching Phase 6") and the median-split bucketing but not the exact statistic formula:** median-split all 772 joined signals on `|call_wall_distance_pct|` into near-wall/far-wall buckets (375/397), `T_obs = day-clustered-mean(far-wall) − day-clustered-mean(near-wall)` (positive = hypothesis confirmed — near-wall suppressed), null built by permuting bucket labels across the same signals (1000 replicates × 2 seeds), one-sided empirical p-value.

| Horizon | T_obs (pp) | p (seed42/43) | Sig. at Bonferroni α=0.0125? | Direction |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5-bar | -0.1183 | 0.835 / 0.822 | No | Reversed |
| **20-bar (primary)** | **-0.1747** | **0.765 / 0.771** | **No** | **Reversed** |
| 10-bar | +0.0003 | 0.482 / 0.493 | No | Nominally correct but ≈0, noise |
| 50-bar | +0.3909 | 0.036 / 0.042 | No (clears uncorrected 0.05, not corrected 0.0125) | Correct direction, but not enough |

**Primary (20-bar) fails all three independent criteria**: not significant (p=0.77 vs. required 0.0125), effect size 0.175pp < 0.5pp threshold, and direction is *reversed* from the hypothesis — near-wall signals showed slightly higher returns than far-wall, not suppressed.

**All 5 declared robustness checks, individually, not rounded to a blanket pass:**
1. **leave_one_ticker_out — FAILS.** Direction reversed under all 6 exclusions, consistently (-0.23pp to -0.36pp every time) — a stable finding, just stable in the wrong direction.
2. **alternate_horizon:5bar — FAILS.** Reversed.
3. **alternate_horizon:10bar — trivially uninformative.** T_obs≈0.0003pp, not a meaningful pass, just noise near zero.
4. **alternate_horizon:50bar — marginal, doesn't clear the bar.** Correct direction, p=0.036-0.042, but only uncorrected-significant, not Bonferroni-significant.
5. **sign_convention:flipped — structurally uninformative, a genuine limitation of this specific declared check, not a bug in the run.** Verified by reading `gex_calculator.py:find_gamma_walls()` (lines 200-216) directly: the function's `sign_convention` parameter is never used in its body — `call_wall_strike`/`put_wall_strike` are computed purely from raw call/put gamma×OI, identical regardless of convention. This check reproduced the primary result byte-for-byte, by construction — it never had the power to independently confirm or refute anything for this specific signal. Worth remembering for any future call-wall/put-wall-based signal: this particular robustness axis doesn't apply to wall-strike features, only to features derived from net GEX or the gamma-flip price, which DO branch on sign_convention (`calculate_net_gex`, `calculate_true_zero_gamma`, `calculate_gamma_gradient_at_spot` all do).

**Stage 1 verdict: REJECTED**, per the pre-registration's literal decision-rule text — *"REJECTED if: Stage 1 N >= 100 but the primary test is not significant at the corrected alpha, OR effect size is below threshold, OR the direction reverses under any declared robustness check."* All three conditions are independently true; this is an unambiguous REJECTED, not a borderline call like IV term structure's 50-day-horizon case.

**EXPERIMENT CLOSED 2026-09-22, per explicit user decision — no Stage 2 run.** A REJECTED Stage 1 closes this experiment under its own pre-specified rules; Stage 2 prospective replication is moot for a hypothesis that already failed on historical data. **`GEX_Prospective_Daily_Collection` (the Windows Scheduled Task feeding `prospective_gex_validation.db` for this specific hypothesis) has been DISABLED** (`Disable-ScheduledTask`, not deleted — confirmed via `Get-ScheduledTask`, `State=Disabled`) since its only purpose was Stage 2 for this now-closed experiment. **CORRECTED 2026-09-26 — this claim is now STALE, discovered during a routine grounding check, not by design.** `Get-ScheduledTask -TaskName "GEX_Prospective_Daily_Collection"` currently shows `State: Ready` (enabled), with a real, successful run logged tonight (2026-09-26 20:00 ET, exit code 0). `prospective_gex_validation.db:raw_signals` has grown from the 11 rows recorded above to **45 rows** (`MAX(trading_date)=2026-09-24`), now covering all 6 tickers (previously 2 had zero rows) — real, substantial, previously-undocumented accumulation, not a one-off. Ticker scope confirmed unchanged (`prospective_gex_pipeline.py:TICKERS` still the original 6 stock tickers, no SPY). **How/when it got re-enabled is unknown — not found in this session's own history, not something this session did.**

**RESOLVED 2026-09-26, explicit user decision: leave it running, as free optionality, not tied to any current experiment.** Checked first whether SPY's pipeline (Section 43) depends on it — it doesn't and explicitly never was designed to (Section 43 quotes its own reasoning: wiring SPY into this task "would have built infrastructure serving the wrong population and the wrong outcome variable"; SPY depends only on `GEX_Historical_Incremental_Update`). With that basis ruled out, the decision to keep it running stands on its own simpler merits: it's cheap, self-healing, does real integrity-checked work nightly, and costs nothing to leave alone even with no destination experiment currently defined — re-enabling it later for a genuinely-designed SPY or other new GEX hypothesis (if one is ever built against its own schema) remains an option, not a requirement. `prospective_gex_validation.db` will keep accumulating (45 rows as of 2026-09-26, all 6 original stock tickers, `MAX(trading_date)=2026-09-24`) with no current consumer — that's accepted, not an oversight. **`GEX_Historical_Incremental_Update` (Section 40) stays running** — it was scoped from the start as general-purpose GEX-cache-maintenance infrastructure, not tied to this one hypothesis, and keeps `gex_metrics` current for whatever GEX-based hypothesis comes next (see Section 42).

**Updated experiment count: GEX call-wall-distance is now this project's second formally-closed hypothesis test after V1 itself** (V1: closed, no edge, Section 18. ML Experiment 1: REJECTED. GEX call-wall-distance: REJECTED. IV term structure: SUGGESTIVE, Stage 2 pending. Strike positioning: not yet run.). Consistent with this project's philosophy (Section 1) — a clean negative result, preserved as-is, not retuned or reframed to look better.

## 37. Strike structure/positioning — candidate signal defined + feasibility confirmed (2026-09-22)

**Roadmap area 5 ("confirmed constructible" but never formalized, Section 6). Scoping/feasibility only, per explicit instruction — no execution, no pre-registration commitment yet.**

**Candidate signal, defined here for the first time, deliberately distinct from every other signal already in this project:**
```
oi_skew = log(total_put_OI_near_money / total_call_OI_near_money)
```
- "Near money" = strikes within ±10% of spot; DTE window 7-45 days (reuses the already-validated near/far bucket range from the IV term-structure work, for comparability).
- **Uses RAW open interest only — no gamma weighting, no IV.** This is the deliberate distinguishing choice: V1 is pure price/volume momentum (no options data at all); GEX's call-wall-distance is gamma-*weighted* hedging-flow mechanics; IV term structure is about *implied-volatility pricing levels*. This candidate is a pure positioning/sentiment proxy — where are traders' contracts actually sitting, unweighted by any pricing model — genuinely answering a different question than any of the other three.
- Hypothesis direction not yet specified (scoping only, per instruction) — plausible candidates for a future pre-registration: elevated put skew as a contrarian or confirming signal, or as a volatility-regime proxy; not decided here.

**Feasibility — checked directly, full universe, id-date-safe, 3 dates spanning V1's window (same dates as the term-structure check, for comparability), not a single-ticker spot check:**

**Result: 18/18 (ticker, date) pairs have real, nonzero open interest on BOTH call and put sides within the DTE 7-45 window.** Coverage is not marginal — hundreds of contracts with nonzero OI per side in every single sample (range: 156-555 contracts per side per ticker-date), total OI in the hundreds of thousands per side for most tickers (NVDA: up to 2.8M total call OI on 6/11). Full detail (per-ticker-date OI counts, totals, strike ranges) saved in the feasibility script's output.

**Conclusion: data fully supports this candidate signal. No coverage gap found at any sampled point.** Ready for a formal pre-registration whenever prioritized — not done in this pass, per instruction.

## 39. Third parallel experiment: strike-structure/positioning (OI skew) — pre-registration drafted (2026-09-22, NOT YET SUBMITTED, NOT RUN)

**Additive to GEX (Section 32) and IV term structure (Section 33), not a replacement — both stay untouched in their own tracks. Formalizes the candidate signal scoped in Section 37.**

**Draft pre-registration: `preregister_strike_positioning_experiment.py`** — same discipline as the other two: constructs a real `ExperimentConfig`, contains no path that can register or execute anything, verified to construct cleanly against the real validator. Genuine `config_hash`: `ae3b3a7df686159c0e3dcad1556f6433c64bee2828a1d2c4bee5cd3b71cded33`. **No data touched — construction and hashing only, per explicit instruction.**

**Signal**: `oi_skew = log(total_put_OI / total_call_OI)`, strikes within ±10% of spot (same-day Alpaca close), summed across all expirations in a 7-45 DTE window, id-date-safe. Raw open interest only — no gamma weighting (unlike GEX), no IV (unlike term structure).

**Hypothesis, made precise and falsifiable rather than "skew matters somehow":** grounded explicitly in classical put/call-ratio *contrarian sentiment* theory, not a dealer-hedging-flow story (that mechanism already belongs to GEX) — elevated put skew reflects crowd hedging/fear, which tends to mean-revert, predicting **higher** subsequent 20-day returns. Low skew (call-heavy, bullish speculation) predicts relatively lower returns. **Opposite sign convention from the IV term-structure test** (there, the predicted correlation is negative; here, positive) — flagged explicitly in the decision rules to avoid confusion between the two.

**Split design: historical two-sided split (Stage 1 pre-V1-window, Stage 2 rolling post-V1-window), matching IV term structure — not GEX-style prospective-only.** Justified directly: this signal reads `open_interest` straight from the raw EODHD chain already confirmed present 2026-01-15 through 2026-09-18+ (Section 33's feasibility work), with no intermediate calculated/stored pipeline required — unlike GEX, which depends on the separately-computed, narrower-window `gex_metrics` table. No unnecessary prospective wait imposed on a signal that doesn't actually need one.

**Stage 2 built rolling from the start** — the hardcoded-literal bug caught and fixed on the IV term-structure experiment (Section 33) was applied proactively here, not repeated: `test_period=None`, `oos_validation_claimed=False`, same stop-on-data-existence mechanism (fixed start 2026-09-10, open end, lock at N≥100).

**5 robustness checks, matching the other two experiments' structure:** leave-one-ticker-out, alternate horizons (10d/50d), alternate moneyness band (±15% vs. ±10%), alternate DTE window (7-30 vs. 7-45).

**Pre-specified decision rules**: VALIDATED requires Stage-1 N≥100, primary 20-day test significant at Bonferroni-corrected α=0.0167 (3 horizons), |correlation|≥0.15 in the **positive** direction, survival of all 5 robustness checks, **and** Stage-2 replication once its rolling window reaches N≥100.

**Status as of 2026-09-22 (updated — GEX call-wall-distance has since CLOSED, Section 32):** IV term structure (Stage 1 executed, SUGGESTIVE, Stage 2 pending, Section 33), strike positioning (drafted, not yet run), GEX net gamma regime (drafted, Section 42, not yet run). Nothing has been submitted to the real framework for this one and no test has run.

### Stage 1 EXECUTED, 2026-09-27 — REJECTED, on three independent grounds simultaneously: primary not significant, effect size far below threshold, and direction reverses under two of five robustness checks.

**Test construction, disclosed explicitly since the pre-registration named the test family but not the literal formula** (same discipline as GEX call-wall-distance, Section 32): pooled Pearson correlation between `oi_skew` and the forward-return horizon under test; null distribution built via a **day-clustered permutation** — a random permutation of unique calendar days (not individual rows), each row's `oi_skew` re-paired with the same ticker's return on the permuted day. One-sided empirical p-value in the hypothesized positive direction. 1000 replicates × 2 seeds (42, 43), matching this project's standard convention.

**Two of the five robustness checks (`alternate_moneyness_band:15pct`, `alternate_dte_window:7-30dte`) required recomputing `oi_skew` with different parameters, not just a different downstream test.** New script `fetch_oi_skew_variants.py` refetched all 605 chains once (not three separate passes) and computed all three variants (primary, band=15%, DTE=7-30) from the same fetch. **Consistency check: the recomputed primary `oi_skew` matched the already-stored value exactly for all 605/605 rows (0 mismatches)** — confirms the refetch is sound before trusting the two alternate variants built from it.

**Primary result (20-day horizon, N=605):**

| Seed | r | p (one-sided) | Sig. at α_corrected=0.0167? |
|---|---|---|---|
| 42 | +0.0248 | 0.0820 | No |
| 43 | +0.0248 | 0.1070 | No |

Correct hypothesized direction (positive/contrarian), but doesn't clear even the *uncorrected* α=0.05 at seed43, let alone the corrected bar. Effect size |r|=0.0248 vs. the required ≥0.15 — misses by roughly 6×.

**5 robustness checks, individually, direction-consistency is this pre-registration's own literal bar for "surviving" (its text: *"Survives ALL 5 declared robustness checks in the SAME DIRECTION"*) — honored as written, not reinterpreted, though both direction and significance are reported for each:**
1. **`leave_one_ticker_out` — FAILS.** 5 of 6 exclusions stay positive (AAPL +0.0104, TSLA +0.0333, NVDA +0.0016, AMD +0.1057, MSTR +0.0732 — AMD's own exclusion is the strongest positive result in this check, p=0.002/0.0000, but still below the 0.15 effect-size floor). **Excluding SMCI reverses the sign to −0.0481** — the aggregate's already-weak positive correlation depends partly on SMCI specifically.
2. **`alternate_horizon:10day` — FAILS, reverses.** r=−0.0136 (both seeds) — the *opposite* sign from the 20-day primary and the hypothesis.
3. **`alternate_horizon:50day` — a real, striking asymmetry, not glossed over.** r=+0.2360, p=0.0000 (both seeds) — clears BOTH the significance bar and the 0.15 effect-size floor by a wide margin, an order of magnitude stronger than the 20-day primary. This does not change the verdict (the pre-registration's primary horizon is 20-day, fixed before any result existed) but is a genuine, disclosed pattern: whatever signal might exist here looks like it operates on a materially longer horizon than the one this experiment was pre-registered to test.
4. **`alternate_moneyness_band:15pct` — direction holds, still fails on strength.** r=+0.0499, p=0.056/0.079 (doesn't clear even uncorrected 0.05 at seed43), still far below the 0.15 effect-size floor.
5. **`alternate_dte_window:7-30dte` — direction technically holds, but trivially.** r=+0.0017 — indistinguishable from zero.

**Stage 1 verdict: REJECTED, unambiguously, on multiple independent triggers** — not a single-check, borderline call like RF v2 v1 (Section 46) or a genuinely ambiguous small-sample case like GEX net gamma regime (Section 42). Primary test fails significance and effect size cleanly; separately, 2 of 5 robustness checks (`leave_one_ticker_out` via SMCI, `alternate_horizon:10day`) reverse direction outright.

**Failure classification (Section 1 taxonomy): genuine signal failure, primary read, with the 50-day-horizon asymmetry flagged as a genuinely different, not-yet-tested question.** The near-money OI-skew signal, aggregated over 7-45 DTE and measured against a 20-day forward return with the pre-registered universe/period, shows no real, robust, direction-stable predictive edge. The 50-day result is real and disclosed, but it was never the pre-registered primary horizon — treating it as vindication would be exactly the kind of post-hoc reframing this project's philosophy (Section 1) exists to prevent. Any future interest in a longer-horizon OI-skew hypothesis would need its own fresh, separately-pre-registered design, not a retroactive promotion of this robustness check.

**Updated running tally: eight closed hypotheses (V1, GEX call-wall-distance, GEX net gamma regime, XGBoost interactions, XGBoost volatility-vs-Ridge v1, Random-Forest volatility-vs-Ridge v1, regularized-XGBoost volatility-vs-Ridge v2, strike-positioning OI-skew), one Stage-1-VALIDATED-pending-Stage-2 (Random-Forest volatility-vs-Ridge v2, Section 48), one SUGGESTIVE-pending-Stage-2 (IV term structure), one scoped-not-run (SPY GEX/volatility).**

**Status: fully executed, documented, CLOSED. No Stage 2 needed** — a REJECTED Stage 1 closes the experiment under its own pre-specified rules, matching this project's established precedent (Sections 32/42/46).

---

## 42. Fourth parallel experiment: GEX net gamma regime — pre-registration drafted (2026-09-22, NOT YET SUBMITTED, NOT RUN)

**Scoped after GEX call-wall-distance CLOSED REJECTED (Section 32). Replaces it as the GEX-family's active track — additive to IV term structure (Section 33) and strike positioning (Section 39), which stay untouched. Construction and hashing only, per explicit instruction — no signal-population join has been run, no relationship between regime and any outcome has been inspected.**

**Draft pre-registration: `preregister_gex_netgammaregime_experiment.py`.** Same discipline as the other three: constructs a real `ExperimentConfig`, contains no path that can register or execute anything, verified to construct cleanly against the real validator. Original `config_hash`: `3c0cd574b84b758395aa23ab68b008b3bace4d382d0c69154a1dde9fea930d13` — **superseded same day, see population-design correction below.** Current `config_hash`: `8dc7b1cd1b1b0f5e5590109106866d4039ef6d9d02f33163465b7cc892bc5f2e`.

**CORRECTED 2026-09-22, same day, caught by explicit user review before Stage 1 was touched — a real population-design flaw, not a nitpick.** The original draft inherited call-wall-distance's V1-qualifying signal population (bullish SPY regime + 20-bar breakout + entry_score≥20, calls only). **This is wrong for this hypothesis specifically**: net gamma regime is a market-structure/volatility-amplification mechanism with no connection to V1's directional bullish setup, and V1's bullish-only gate was very plausibly suppressing exactly the negative-regime days this hypothesis needs (negative dealer gamma is theorized to correlate with market stress/decline — the opposite of V1's bullish requirement). Worse structurally: `gex_regime` is itself a once-per-ticker-per-day quantity, so multiple intraday V1 signals on the same day all share the *identical* regime value — day-clustered statistical power depends on *distinct days represented*, not signal count, and the day-level ceiling for negative-regime days (41 in the full table) can never be exceeded by adding more intraday signals on top of it.

**Fixed: population is now day-level and unconditional on V1's gate** — one observation per (ticker, trading day), anchored at that day's market-open bar, no bullish-regime/breakout/entry-score/calls-only requirement at all. This matches an **already-established precedent in this exact project**: `iv_term_structure_slope_v1_20260921`'s own scope note explicitly chose the same "NOT conditioned on V1's entry signal... population is all valid trading days... independent of whether V1 would have fired an entry that day" design (Section 33), for the identical underlying reason — a market-structure mechanism, not a directional-entry-conditional one.

**Verified before locking, not assumed favorable — direct query against the current table:** Stage 1 window (2026-06-10 to 2026-08-11) under the new day-level population has **260 total (ticker,day) rows, 33 negative-regime (12.7%)** — comfortably clears both `min_n_per_stage=100` and `min_n_negative_regime_per_stage=20`. Stage 2 (so far, still growing) has 165 total, 8 negative — clears the total floor, not yet the negative-regime floor, consistent with the general still-accumulating pattern already established for the other rolling-Stage-2 experiments in this family. This is a materially better-powered design than the superseded V1-gated version, not just a theoretically cleaner one.

**This is the same signal construction SpyGammaLoop already uses live for entry gating (Sections 34-36) — but SpyGammaLoop's use of it has never been formally tested for predictiveness, only used operationally on live paper capital. This is the first formal predictiveness test of that construction.**

**Signal: `gex_regime` (positive/negative), the sign of aggregate T-1 dealer gamma exposure** (`gex_lookup.py:get_gex_for_signal`, `sign_convention=model_a`) — a categorical, portfolio-wide feature, not tied to any specific strike.

**Explicitly verified distinct from the closed call-wall-distance hypothesis, not a relabeling of it:**
- Call-wall-distance = **spatial/positional, first-moment**: proximity to *one specific strike* predicting *suppressed return level* (a localized pinning mechanism). Built on `find_gamma_walls()`, confirmed by direct code read to **not** branch on `sign_convention` at all (Section 32's finding).
- Net gamma regime = **aggregate, second-moment**: the *sign of total dealer exposure* (no strike information) predicting *forward realized volatility magnitude*, sign-agnostic on direction (a portfolio-wide hedging-flow mechanism). Built on `calculate_net_gex()`, confirmed by direct code read to **flip on `sign_convention`** (`net = call_gex − put_gex` under `model_a`, `put_gex − call_gex` under `flipped` — `gex_calculator.py:109-112`), consistent with the observed near-total mirror-image regime counts between the two conventions.
- Different functions, different outcome variables (return level vs. volatility magnitude), different theories (localized pinning vs. portfolio-wide amplification). Not assumed correlated with call-wall-distance — that correlation, if any, was not computed.

**Hypothesis (directional, falsifiable):** among all valid (ticker, trading day) observations in the 6-ticker universe — day-level, unconditional on V1's gate (see correction above) — negative net gamma regime at T-1 predicts systematically *higher* forward realized volatility (measured from that day's market open) than positive regime — the standard "net-short-gamma dealers hedge in the same direction as the move, amplifying it" theory, vs. positive regime's dampening effect.

**Feasibility, day-level and unconditional (current design):** Stage 1 window: 260 total, 33 negative (12.7%). Stage 2 window so far: 165 total, 8 negative. (Raw full-table reference figure, unconditional on stage, what the now-superseded V1-gated design was working from: 384 positive/41 negative of 425 rows, 9.6% negative.) **`flipped` convention is a near-total mirror image** of `model_a`'s regime counts — confirms `sign_convention:flipped` is a *meaningful* robustness check for this hypothesis, unlike its confirmed no-op status for call-wall-distance.

**Remaining, smaller disclosed risk, not fully eliminated by the population fix:** the severe per-ticker imbalance in negative-regime days (SMCI=1, AAPL=2 in the full table) still means leave-one-ticker-out robustness could be materially affected by excluding AMD/MSTR/TSLA specifically, which carry most of the negative-regime days. **Deliberate addition not present in the other three experiments:** a minority-class floor, `min_n_negative_regime_per_stage=20`, alongside the usual `min_n_per_stage=100` total floor — a technically-adequate total N with too few negative-regime observations does not count as sufficient.

**Split design, necessarily different from IV term structure/strike positioning:** GEX has no data before 2026-06-10 (`gex_data_loader.py`'s own frozen `TRAIN_START`) — a pre-V1-window historical split isn't available for any GEX-based hypothesis. Instead, Stage 1/Stage 2 split at `gex_data_loader.py`'s own **already-existing** `TRAIN_END=2026-08-11` constant (a non-arbitrary boundary that predates this experiment, not chosen or tuned for it). Stage 1 = 2026-06-10 to 2026-08-11. Stage 2 = rolling, fixed start 2026-08-12, open end via a data-existence check only, growing through the already-running, general-purpose `GEX_Historical_Incremental_Update` job (Section 40/32) — **no new prospective/forward-collection pipeline needed**, unlike the now-closed call-wall-distance design. `test_period=None`, `oos_validation_claimed=False`, matching the now-established rolling-Stage-2 pattern from IV term structure/strike positioning.

**Outcome metric, pinned upfront (unlike IV term structure, which had to fix this mid-stream after an ambiguity was caught):** forward realized volatility = population stdev of consecutive 5-minute-bar log returns over the horizon, measured from each ticker-day's market-open anchor bar, not annualized (comparisons are relative, so a constant annualization scalar wouldn't change any conclusion). Deliberately 5-minute-bar-based, not IV term structure's daily-bar formula — matches this project's intraday data granularity. The market-open anchor is itself part of the population correction above — a non-arbitrary, once-per-day reference point matching `gex_regime`'s own once-per-ticker-per-day granularity.

**Effect size, deliberately a ratio not a percentage-point gap:** negative-regime forward realized vol must be ≥1.20× (20% relatively higher than) positive-regime forward realized vol. Volatility comparisons are naturally multiplicative, not additive — a pp-based threshold (matching the other three experiments' convention) would be a category error here, not a meaningful adaptation.

**5 robustness checks:** leave-one-ticker-out (flagged as higher-risk than the other experiments' version, given the per-ticker imbalance above), alternate horizons (5d/10d/50d), sign_convention:flipped (genuinely meaningful here, unlike call-wall-distance's no-op version).

**Pre-specified decision rules**: VALIDATED requires Stage-1 N≥100 **and** negative-regime N≥20, primary 20-bar test significant at Bonferroni-corrected α=0.0125 (4 horizons), effect ratio ≥1.20×, survival of all 5 robustness checks, **and** Stage-2 replication once its own N/negative-N thresholds are reached.

---

### Stage 1 EXECUTED, 2026-09-22 — REJECTED. Experiment CLOSED, no Stage 2. Correct direction, insufficient strength — a distinct flavor of rejection from call-wall-distance's wrong-direction result.

**Joined N = 252** (negative=33, positive=219; 258 raw day-level observations → 252 after GEX-availability filtering). **42 distinct days overall, 18 distinct negative-regime days.** Both pre-specified floors cleared: `min_n_per_stage=100` ✓, `min_n_negative_regime_per_stage=20` ✓ — the population-redesign correction above did its job; this would very likely have been INCONCLUSIVE (insufficient minority-class N) under the original, superseded V1-gated design.

| Horizon | T_obs | ratio (neg/pos) | p (seed42/43) | Sig. at α=0.0125? | Direction |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5-bar | +0.000339 | 1.067× | 0.297 / 0.291 | No | Correct, weak |
| 10-bar | +0.000548 | 1.106× | 0.166 / 0.175 | No | Correct, weak |
| **20-bar (primary)** | **+0.00079** | **1.167×** | **0.044 / 0.052** | **No** | **Correct, but short** |
| 50-bar | +0.00068 | 1.188× | 0.025 / 0.018 | No (closest of the four) | Correct |

**Primary (20-bar): direction correct** (negative-regime realized vol 0.005519 > positive-regime 0.004729) **but fails both other independent criteria** — not significant at the corrected bar (seed42=0.044 clears only the *uncorrected* 0.05; seed43=0.052 doesn't even clear that), and effect ratio 1.167× falls just short of the 1.20× threshold.

**5 robustness checks, individually, not rounded up:**
1. **leave_one_ticker_out — direction-consistent but magnitude-fragile.** All 6 exclusions stay positive-direction (ratios 1.01×–1.30×), never reversing — but excluding MSTR (1.010×) or AAPL (1.069×) nearly zeroes the effect out.
2. **alternate_horizon:5bar — direction correct, not meaningfully passing.** p≈0.29 both seeds.
3. **alternate_horizon:10bar — direction correct, not meaningfully passing.** p≈0.17 both seeds.
4. **alternate_horizon:50bar — direction correct, closest to significant of anything in this run**, still short of the corrected bar (p=0.018–0.025 vs. required 0.0125).
5. **sign_convention:flipped — FAILS, direction reverses** (T_obs=-0.00079, ratio=0.857×, p=0.955/0.944). **Correction to this section's own earlier characterization, discovered only by actually running it:** the design writeup above predicted this would be "genuinely meaningful here, unlike call-wall-distance's no-op." Having run it, that's imprecise — `flipped` produces an almost-exact label swap of the same 252 ticker-days (33/219 → 219/33 exactly), so this check is close to a mirror-image restatement of the primary result rather than an independent replication: whenever `model_a` shows *any* nonzero-direction effect, `flipped` is nearly mechanically guaranteed to show the reverse. A different kind of limitation than call-wall-distance's byte-identical no-op, but still a real limitation on how much independent information this specific check contributes for a regime-*sign* feature (as opposed to a wall-*strike* feature, where sign_convention genuinely doesn't apply at all). The literal decision-rule text doesn't carve out an exception for this, so it counts as a REJECTED trigger as written.

**Stage 1 verdict: REJECTED.** Per the pre-registration's literal text, all three independent REJECTED triggers fire simultaneously: primary not significant at corrected α, effect size below the 1.20× threshold, and direction reverses under a declared robustness check.

**Failure classification (per Section 1's refined taxonomy): primarily INSUFFICIENT EVIDENCE, with GENUINE SIGNAL FAILURE equally plausible — not forced into one bucket.** Unlike call-wall-distance (wrong direction throughout — a clean genuine-signal-failure read), this result is genuinely ambiguous by nature, not just by convention: direction was correct everywhere it was tested (primary, all 3 secondary horizons, all 6 leave-one-ticker-out exclusions), and the 50-bar horizon came within striking distance of the corrected bar (p=0.018 vs. 0.0125 needed). With only 18 distinct negative-regime days in Stage 1, this is a genuinely small sample for detecting a subtle volatility-amplification effect — plausibly a real but small effect that this Stage 1 window lacked the power to confirm, or plausibly no real effect at all beyond noise that happened to lean the hypothesized direction. The formal decision-rule verdict is unambiguously REJECTED regardless of which causal story is right; this note records the honest uncertainty about *why*, separate from the formal verdict itself.

**EXPERIMENT CLOSED 2026-09-22.** No Stage 2 run — a REJECTED Stage 1 closes the experiment under its own pre-specified rules. **`GEX_Historical_Incremental_Update` (Section 40) marked INACTIVE/DEFERRED, not deleted or modified** — disabled via `Disable-ScheduledTask` (confirmed `State=Disabled`), no data touched. With this closure, no active GEX hypothesis currently depends on it; it can be re-enabled instantly if a future GEX hypothesis needs fresh historical data. `gex_metrics` itself (850+ rows, 2026-06-10 to 2026-09-21) stays exactly as it is, untouched, available for any future GEX work without needing to be rebuilt. **UPDATE 2026-09-23/24 (Section 43): re-enabled** — a new SPY-specific hypothesis now depends on it (for the 6-stock tickers only; SPY has its own separate, one-time, unscheduled backfill script).

**Running tally: three closed hypotheses (V1, GEX call-wall-distance, GEX net gamma regime), one SUGGESTIVE-pending-Stage-2 (IV term structure), two scoped-not-run (strike positioning, SPY GEX/volatility — Section 43).** Per Section 1's updated framing — this is the research process working correctly, not a string of failures to be worried about.

---

## 44. Sixth formal experiment: XGBoost feature-interaction hypothesis — pre-registration drafted (2026-09-24, NOT YET SUBMITTED, NOT RUN)

**First new experiment outside the GEX/IV/strike-positioning family — the second ML-track experiment after the rejected `ml_experiment1_feature_factory_vs_baseline_20260917`.** `preregister_xgboost_interaction_experiment.py`. Same discipline as every other pre-registration: constructs a real `ExperimentConfig`, no path to `preregister_experiment()`/`run_experiment()` anywhere, verified to construct cleanly. **Hash history: `026e5f997f423e38cfc788f736cee4e3e1082e634f7f49e1d2578caccd14d364` (original, realized-vol-proxy Greeks) → `35b5e9b722d3f87928819a29dd0c72584c3d9d0d9b696e135a34f555409fd31f` (current, 2026-09-24 — real-IV swap, see correction below).**

**CORRECTED 2026-09-24, same day, per explicit user instruction — the Greeks' volatility input was swapped from V1's realized-vol proxy to real historical EODHD IV before this pre-registration was ever finalized.** `delta`/`gamma`/`theta`/`charm`/`vega_per_1pct_iv` will now be computed via the already-validated `v2_iv_pricing_model.py:get_v2_entry_iv(ticker, entry_timestamp, strike, option_type, target_dte=14)` — the same T-1-safe, id-date-safe, 8.8%-median-error methodology the V1/V2 pricing-correction chain already uses (Sections 3-4, 17) — instead of `calculate_historical_volatility()`. `realized_volatility`/`realized_vol_percentile` are unchanged (genuinely different, realized- not implied-volatility features, not part of the swap). **Consequence, disclosed not hidden:** trades whose exact strike/DTE has no matching real quoted contract on entry date are now EXCLUDED, never substituted with the proxy — final joined N will likely be somewhat lower than a proxy-based count would have produced, exact figure not yet known (data-generation pass not yet run). **Disclosed cost estimate**: ~2,500-3,500 additional EODHD API calls (one per qualifying trade entry, not per bar), the same order of magnitude as several single passes already completed this session (SPY historical backfill, strike-positioning Stage 1, both 600+ calls) — feasible, not a blocking constraint. Both the module docstring and the `parameters` dict (`data_generation_source`, `greeks_volatility_input`, `greeks_swap_cost_disclosure` — this last field newly added) were updated to reflect this; the old `greeks_volatility_input_disclosure` field (which stated the proxy limitation) no longer exists in the current config.

**Real data-depth test performed empirically before any design decision — not assumed from EODHD's own "robust two-year history" marketing claim:**
- **EODHD options data: a genuine, hard cliff at exactly 2023-10-02**, confirmed by bisection across all 6 project tickers plus SPY (not a single spot-check) — 2023-09-29 (a real Friday trading day) returns zero id-date-matched records for all 7 tickers; 2023-10-02 (the next trading day) returns full, populated data (`volatility` field present) for all 7. This is **earlier (better) than the marketed two years** — 2023-10-02 to today is just under **three years**. Two false alarms were caught and corrected during the search, not glossed over: an initial naive bisection hit 2024-01-15 (MLK Day, a market holiday) and 2024-01-20 (a Saturday) and briefly misread these as depth-limit signals before recognizing them as ordinary non-trading-day artifacts once cross-checked against real weekday dates.
- **Alpaca daily bars: uniform 2020-07-27 start across all 6 tickers** (confirmed via the same construction `backtest_engine.get_daily_regime()` uses internally), extending to 2026-09-23. **Not the binding constraint** — EODHD's options depth is, since every feature in this experiment requires options Greeks/OI, not just underlying price.
- **Real, confirmed usable window: 2023-10-02 to today, ~2.97 years, all 6 tickers** — not the 90-day default `generate_training_data.py` currently uses (confirmed to be a self-imposed convenience default in that script, not a real data constraint). A new data-generation pass over this full window is required; the existing 573-row `ml_training_data.db` is not reused.

**Genuinely distinct from ML Experiment 1's REJECTED verdict, confirmed by direct code read, not assumed:**
- ML Experiment 1's `LOCKED_FEATURES` (`ml_experiment1_executor.py`): `past_return_n, breakout_strength, atr_normalized, dte_at_index, delta_at_index, iv_vs_realized_vol, iv_percentile, spy_trend_state, relative_strength_vs_spy, vwap_distance` — predicting **20-bar forward returns** via Ridge.
- This experiment's feature set (`feature_extraction.py`'s 13 features, confirmed a **completely different, non-overlapping** set): delta, gamma, theta, charm, vega_per_1pct_iv, realized_volatility(+percentile), relative_volume, breakout/trend/momentum scores, moneyness, dte — predicting **trade outcome (win/loss)** on the already-validated V1-style entry/exit chain.
- Different features, different target, different population, different model class, and the actual hypothesis under test — named interactions — isolated by holding model class constant (XGBoost-without-interactions vs. XGBoost-with-interactions), not "trees beat linear models generally." ML Experiment 1's rejection cannot structurally transfer to this hypothesis (Section 19 discipline — a rejection doesn't transfer to a non-identical hypothesis).

**3 named interactions, each with a stated, falsifiable theoretical reason** (not "trees find interactions automatically"):
1. **`realized_volatility × relative_volume`** ("high-conviction move" theory) — either factor alone can be noise (a thin illiquid print, or volume with no directional conviction); their joint elevation signals genuine broad-participation conviction. `entry_scorer.py` already scores both, but additively — this tests whether their *product*, not their sum, carries information.
2. **`gamma × moneyness`** ("convexity regime-dependence" theory) — gamma's practical P&L-sensitivity impact depends on where in moneyness-space the position sits; a linear model forces gamma's coefficient constant across all moneyness levels, an explicitly conditional not additive relationship.
3. **`breakout_magnitude_score × trend_strength_score`** ("confirmation vs. exhaustion" theory) — a strong breakout without trend context is a classic false/exhaustion pattern; the same breakout score with strong trend context is a continuation signal — the same value should predict oppositely depending on the other factor.

~~**Honest disclosure, not glossed over:** the Greeks in this feature set are computed via `calculate_historical_volatility()` (V1's own realized-vol proxy), **not real EODHD-observed IV** — a known, disclosed limitation inherited from `feature_extraction.py`'s existing construction, not introduced by this experiment.~~ **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-24 — see the correction above.** The Greeks now use real EODHD-observed historical IV via `v2_iv_pricing_model.py:get_v2_entry_iv()`, not the proxy. Struck through rather than deleted, per this project's own convention (Section 14), since it was a real, accurate statement about the config as it existed at draft time.

**Design, modeled on `preregister_spy_gex_volatility_experiment.py`'s structure (as instructed) plus `ml_experiment1_final_executor.py`'s locked-primary-test-set discipline:**
- **Primary comparison**: XGBoost on 13 base features (Model A) vs. XGBoost on 13 base + 3 interaction features (Model B) — model class held constant to isolate the interactions' own contribution.
- **Real regularization, not defaults**: `max_depth` capped at 3, `min_child_weight≥5`, nonzero L1/L2, row/column subsampling ≤0.8, tuned via nested cross-validation (train+val only, test never touches hyperparameter selection) — the dataset remains modest even at ~3 years, so the model must fight overfitting by design.
- **Primary test**: paired one-sided Wilcoxon on per-observation log-loss (mirrors ML Experiment 1's MSE-based Wilcoxon exactly, substituting log-loss for probabilistic classification), single primary comparison, no Bonferroni family needed (one comparison, not multiple horizons) — matching ML Experiment 1's own actual structure, not the GEX-family's multi-horizon design.
- **Effect size floor**: AUC-ROC(B) − AUC-ROC(A) ≥ 0.05, a stated convention not a literature mandate.
- **Locked primary test set, mechanism pinned now, not a copied fixed count**: chronological 60/20/20 train/val/test split by entry date (purged), with the primary significance test using a deterministically-thinned non-overlapping subset (first qualifying trade per ticker per calendar week within the test period) — the full untinned test set is descriptive only, mirroring ML Experiment 1's own full-vs-primary-test-set split.
- **5 robustness checks**: `alternate_model:random_forest` (mandatory per instruction — Random Forest chosen over CatBoost since CatBoost has zero existing precedent in this codebase, confirmed by grep; if XGBoost shows the effect and Random Forest doesn't, treat as noise, same severity as this project's other alternate-model checks), `leave_one_interaction_out` (3 sub-checks, confirms no single interaction is carrying the whole effect), `leave_one_ticker_out`, `alternate_horizon:exit_reason_stratified` (reversal_guard vs. stop_loss subgroups, given Section 13's own finding these behave near-oppositely), and `shuffled_target_placebo` — a genuinely different-shaped check (target randomly permuted; should show *no* effect; if it *does*, that indicates a pipeline/leakage bug, not that the hypothesis is wrong) included specifically because none of the other four checks could catch that failure mode. Its pass condition is the *inverse* of the other four's, stated explicitly to avoid the interpretation ambiguity this project already hit once (Section 33's 50-day-horizon case).
- **`concentration_check` included in `applicability_lock`**, relevant here in a way it wasn't for the single-signal GEX-family experiments — mirrors Phase 7's concentration discipline (Section 13), checking whether one ticker or sub-period drives the entire primary-test effect.

**Placeholder note, disclosed explicitly**: `train_period`/`validation_period`/`test_period` literal date boundaries are a first-pass 60/20/20 split of the confirmed window, pending the actual joined trade count from the new data-generation pass — re-verify and adjust proportionally once real N is known, rather than silently trusting these literals the way earlier sessions' pre-registrations initially trusted unverified literals.

**Data-generation pass complete, 2026-09-25 — real dataset built, Stage 1 still NOT run.** New script `generate_xgboost_interaction_dataset.py`, reusing `run_options_backtest(..., collect_features=True)` unmodified (same mechanism `generate_training_data.py` uses) over the confirmed 2023-10-02-to-today window, all 6 tickers, calls-only (matching this baseline's existing convention). For each of the resulting raw trades, looked up real historical EODHD IV via `v2_iv_pricing_model.py:get_v2_entry_iv()` and recomputed the five Greeks with it (realized_volatility/realized_vol_percentile left untouched), then computed the 3 named interaction features on the swapped base set. Saved to a **brand-new** database, `ml_xgboost_interaction_data.db` (`xgboost_training_examples` + `excluded_trades` tables) — `generate_training_data.py`'s existing `ml_training_data.db` (573 rows/90 days) was never touched.

**Two real bugs surfaced during this run, both found, root-caused, and fixed before the final N was accepted — not rounded up or glossed over:**
1. **Dedup-key collision, first run.** The resumability key `(ticker, entry_date, exit_date)` is unsafe: a ticker can complete more than one full round-trip on the same calendar day (5-minute-bar granularity + `reversal_guard`/`stop_loss` exits make this common), and two genuinely distinct trades sharing that key get treated as duplicates. First full run produced 2,618 raw trades but only 2,580 saved + 19 excluded = 2,599 accounted for — **19 trades silently dropped**, confirmed by a direct diagnostic rerun of all 6 backtests: exactly 19 colliding `(entry_date, exit_date)` pairs, `strike` distinct in every single one (e.g. AAPL 2025-08-06 had two separate reversal_guard-exited trades that day, strikes 212.73 and 217.09). **Fixed**: dedup key now includes `strike` (plus `pnl_dollars` for the saved-set specifically), added a `strike` column to `excluded_trades` via `ALTER TABLE`. Verified after the fix — all 19 previously-dropped trades were recovered (18 saved, matching real IV; a handful independently re-confirmed excluded where applicable), with zero overlap between saved/excluded.
2. **Duplicate concurrent process launch, second run.** The corrected rerun's log shows two `RUN START` entries 48 seconds apart (02:52:40 and 02:53:28 UTC) — the script was somehow invoked twice concurrently (first instance died mid-AAPL, before any commit; second instance ran to completion), and since each process loads its own in-memory seen-set once at startup, both independently reprocessed the same 19 recovered trades, producing duplicate inserts for 5 of 6 tickers (MSTR happened to be unaffected). Caught via a direct DB integrity check (`COUNT(*)` vs. `COUNT(DISTINCT ...)` mismatch: 2,613 saved rows but only 2,599 distinct `(ticker, entry_date, exit_date, strike)` combinations). **Fixed** with a one-time cleanup pass: `DELETE ... WHERE id NOT IN (SELECT MIN(id) ... GROUP BY ...)` on both tables, keeping the earliest row per genuine trade.

**Final, verified dataset state (post-cleanup, integrity-checked, not just trusted from the run's own summary print):**
- **2,599 rows saved** to `xgboost_training_examples` (real-IV Greeks + 3 interaction features, ready for the eventual Stage 1 join).
- **19 rows excluded** to `excluded_trades`, all reason `no_real_contract_at_t_minus_1` — i.e. **99.3% real-IV match rate** (2,599/2,618), consistent with this project's other real-IV match rates elsewhere (94.4%-98.8%, Section 3/17) and considerably better than the pre-swap disclosure's "somewhat lower" hedge — the exclusion cost of the real-IV swap turned out to be small in practice, not the dominant effect on final N.
- **2,599 + 19 = 2,618 exactly** — matches the raw trade count from the 6-ticker backtest with zero unaccounted trades, zero remaining duplicate groups, zero saved/excluded overlap (all independently re-verified via direct SQL after the cleanup, not assumed).
- Per-ticker saved/excluded: AAPL 398/6, TSLA 455/3, NVDA 439/5, AMD 452/1, SMCI 421/2, MSTR 434/2.

**Config hash re-confirmed unchanged after the full data-generation pass**: `35b5e9b722d3f87928819a29dd0c72584c3d9d0d9b696e135a34f555409fd31f` — dataset generation never touches the `ExperimentConfig` object, so no drift was possible, but reconfirmed fresh anyway rather than assumed.

---

### Stage 1 EXECUTED, 2026-09-25 — REJECTED. Not submitted to the real framework (consistent with every other GEX/IV/strike-family "Stage 1 EXECUTED" section — direct analysis script + this record, not `preregister_experiment()`/`run_experiment()`, which only ML Experiment 1 has ever gone through).

**Operational note, disclosed because it affected which data was fit, not just a footnote:** the PowerShell background-execution tool used this session silently double-launched a script as two genuinely concurrent OS processes on two separate occasions — once during the dataset-generation rerun (caught via two `RUN START` log entries 48s apart) and once again caught *live* mid-Stage-1 (the DB grew from a verified 2,599 to 2,604 rows while Stage 1 was already running on a stale in-memory snapshot). Both times: killed the stale process, re-ran a `DELETE ... WHERE id NOT IN (SELECT MIN(id) ... GROUP BY ...)` cleanup keyed on `(ticker, entry_date, exit_date, strike)`, verified zero remaining duplicate groups and zero saved/excluded overlap via direct SQL before trusting anything downstream. **Final, clean dataset used for the reported Stage 1 result below: 2,599 rows** (verified stable at this count after the second cleanup, confirmed via a fresh process-list check showing no stray python processes remained).

**Model A = XGBoost, 13 base features. Model B = XGBoost, 13 base + 3 named interaction features. There is no Ridge arm in this design** — the locked pre-registration's own text is explicit: *"This is testing the specific interaction terms, not 'XGBoost is generally better than Ridge' — the comparison holds model class constant (XGBoost vs. XGBoost)."* Ridge belongs to the separate, already-REJECTED `ml_experiment1_feature_factory_vs_baseline_20260917`.

**Setup**: chronological 60/20/20 split by real trade count (not the pre-registration's placeholder calendar literals, per its own explicit instruction to override them once real N was known) — train=1,563 (2023-10-03 to 2025-08-01), val=520 (to 2026-02-25), test=516 (to 2026-09-24). **Primary (locked, non-overlapping) test set: 182 observations** (first trade per ticker per ISO calendar week within the test period, exactly the pre-registration's locked mechanism) — clears `min_n_per_stage=100`. XGBoost tuned via a real 64-combination nested-CV grid (`max_depth∈{2,3}, min_child_weight∈{5,10}, reg_alpha∈{0.1,1.0}, reg_lambda∈{1.0,5.0}, subsample∈{0.7,0.8}, colsample_bytree∈{0.7,0.8}`, TimeSeriesSplit 5-fold, selected by mean CV log-loss) on train+val only; test set never touched hyperparameter selection.

**Primary result — significant at neither seed, and sign-unstable across seeds:**

| Seed | AUC A | AUC B | AUC delta | Wilcoxon p (one-sided) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 42 | 0.5472 | 0.5552 | +0.0081 | 0.351 |
| 43 | 0.5483 | 0.5446 | **−0.0037** | 0.079 |

Both models barely above chance regardless (AUC 0.52-0.55). Effect size threshold (≥0.05) missed by ~6x in the better seed; wrong sign in the other.

**5 robustness checks, individually, none rounded up:**
1. **`alternate_model:random_forest` — genuine disagreement, not a footnote.** RF: AUC delta = **−0.0007** (essentially zero). Random Forest does **not** corroborate XGBoost's already-marginal, seed-unstable result.
2. **`leave_one_interaction_out` — mixed, not one clean culprit.** Dropping `realized_volatility_x_relative_volume`: delta vs. A still +0.0070. Dropping `gamma_x_moneyness`: delta vs. A goes **negative** (−0.0021). Dropping `breakout_magnitude_score_x_trend_strength_score`: delta vs. A **negative** (−0.0102). Two of three ablations flip sign.
3. **`leave_one_ticker_out` — direction stays nominally positive in all 6 exclusions, but reveals real heterogeneity.** Excluding AMD alone produces the strongest sub-result (delta +0.071, p=0.011) — AMD's own data is what drags the full-population effect toward zero, not flagged by the mechanical rule but worth naming.
4. **`alternate_horizon:exit_reason_stratified` — structurally uninformative for this population, confirmed directly, not assumed.** Queried the DB: **100% of `reversal_guard` exits have `won=1`; 100% of `stop_loss` exits have `won=0`** — zero within-stratum class variance (the same near-definitional win-rate fact Section 13 already documented for V1's own exit structure, now confirmed to break this specific check too). AUC is mathematically undefined (NaN) in each stratum. Same failure class as call-wall-distance's `sign_convention` no-op (Section 32) — flagged as a broken check, not counted as independent evidence of reversal.
5. **`shuffled_target_placebo` — clean, no leakage.** AUC delta on randomly-shuffled training labels: +0.0124, p=0.573 (not significant). The pipeline itself is sound; the weak real-data result is not a plumbing artifact.

**Concentration check**: no single ticker or chronological half of the primary test set dominates the (already tiny) effect — SMCI contributes most positively (+0.021 mean log-loss diff), AMD and TSLA are actually negative, first/second halves similar magnitude. Consistent with noise, not a hidden concentrated signal.

**Verdict: REJECTED**, on multiple independent clean grounds that hold even discounting the broken stratified check — primary test not significant at either seed, effect size not met at either seed (sign-unstable), Random Forest does not corroborate XGBoost. **Failure classification (Section 1 taxonomy): genuine signal failure** — these three specific named interactions do not carry predictive information about trade outcome beyond the 13 base features, for this model class, on this population. Not data-limited (N=182 primary/2,599 total was ample) or model-limited (regularized nested-CV tuning, corroborating-model check, and a clean leakage check were all in place) — a real negative result on a well-specified, falsifiable hypothesis.

**Updated running tally: four closed hypotheses (V1, GEX call-wall-distance, GEX net gamma regime, XGBoost feature interactions), one SUGGESTIVE-pending-Stage-2 (IV term structure), two scoped-not-run (strike positioning, SPY GEX/volatility).** Per Section 1's framing — the research process working correctly, not a string of failures.

**Status: fully executed, documented, closed. No further action on this hypothesis** unless a genuinely new interaction/feature construction is proposed as a distinct future hypothesis (per Section 19 discipline — this rejection does not transfer to a non-identical hypothesis, e.g. a different model class, different interactions, or a different target).
