## 45. Seventh formal experiment: XGBoost vs. Ridge on forward realized volatility — pre-registration drafted (2026-09-25, NOT YET SUBMITTED, NOT RUN)

**Standalone, NOT a continuation of the closed/REJECTED `xgboost_feature_interactions_v1_20260924` (Section 44).** `preregister_xgboost_volatility_experiment.py`. Same discipline as every pre-registration in this project: constructs a real `ExperimentConfig`, no path to `preregister_experiment()`/`run_experiment()`, verified to construct cleanly. **`config_hash`: `76f200b96fdabb10bb0af2edfc12e4a098abf664108e4349711ed70f0b3ea04b`.**

**Why this target, explicitly**: three separate modeling attempts on a win/loss or directional target have now failed in this project — V1's raw signal (Phase 6), ML Experiment 1's Feature Factory+Ridge (REJECTED), and the XGBoost interaction experiment (REJECTED, Section 44). The one target class with a real, surviving, pre-registered signal is **forward realized volatility** (`iv_term_structure_slope_v1_20260921`, Section 33 — Stage 1 correlation -0.28 to -0.31, p=0.001-0.002, robust). This experiment targets that same class with a richer feature set and a nonlinear model, rather than another attempt at the demonstrated-hard directional class.

**Explicit non-interference constraint**: does not touch, read, or write IV term structure's own Stage 2 rolling-accumulation pipeline (Section 33) or `prospective_gex_validation.db` — fully separate dataset build, database, and hash. Only the term-structure-slope feature *definition* (bucket DTEs, expiration-selection convention, matched-strike tiebreak, realized-vol formula) is reused verbatim, for comparability, not shared data.

**Feature-depth verification, performed before locking the design, not assumed from a prior session's partial check:**
- **GEX metrics**: still only 2026-06-10 to 2026-09-23 (~3.5 months, confirms Section 40's `TRAIN_START` constant unchanged).
- **Term-structure (near/far IV buckets)**: broadly feasible at 2023-10-02/2024-06-03/2025-06-02 spot-checks, all 6 tickers, **except NVDA and MSTR both showed 0 near-bucket expirations at 2024-06-03 on a single-page query** — flagged as needing the full paginated fetch method (not this quick check) when the real data-generation pass runs; some ticker-days will legitimately be excluded, never substituted.
- **OI skew**: robustly populated at all 3 spot-check dates, extending Section 37's 2026-window confirmation back to 2023-10-02.

**Decision, explicitly documented: GEX features are EXCLUDED from this design.** Including them would cost an ~89% reduction in usable window (3 years → 3.5 months) for a feature class whose own standalone predictive value for this exact target was already formally tested and REJECTED (`net_gamma_regime`, Section 42 — correct direction throughout, but effect ratio 1.167× fell short of the 1.20× threshold on only 18 negative-regime days). Not worth the N cost for an unproven feature.

**Design**:
- **8 features**: `term_structure_slope_pct`, `oi_skew`, `trailing_realized_vol_20d` (a new, legitimate lagged-volatility feature added so the baseline isn't artificially weak — backward-looking, no lookahead), `delta`/`gamma`/`theta`/`charm`/`vega_per_1pct_iv` (real-IV Greeks at the same near-bucket matched-strike contract already fetched for term structure).
- **Model A = Ridge regression** (not plain OLS) — chosen because several features are genuinely correlated (gamma/vega with moneyness-DTE, trailing vol with Greeks), and OLS would be an unfairly unstable/weak baseline; Ridge keeps the comparison linear while giving Model A a real chance, isolating nonlinearity as the one tested variable. **Model B = XGBoost regression**, same 8 features, same regularization discipline as the interaction experiment (max_depth≤3, min_child_weight≥5, nonzero L1/L2, subsample/colsample≤0.8, nested CV on train+val only).
- **Primary test**: paired one-sided Wilcoxon on per-observation **absolute** error (not squared — more robust to volatility-spike outlier days over a 3-year window). Bonferroni family of 3 horizons (10/20/50-day), α_corrected=0.0167, reused from IV term structure's own convention.
- **Effect-size floor**: R²(B) − R²(A) ≥ 0.05 — the regression analog of the interaction experiment's AUC-delta=0.05 convention.
- **Primary test-set construction**: LOCKED, non-overlapping, same mechanism as `ml_experiment1_final_executor.py`/the interaction experiment (first observation per ticker per ISO week within the test period) — deliberately **not** IV term structure's day-clustered Monte Carlo design, per the explicit design requirement for this experiment.
- **5 robustness checks**: `shuffled_target_placebo` (now **mandatory** per a new Section 1 rule, added this session — see below), `leave_one_ticker_out`, `alternate_model:random_forest`, `alternate_horizon:10day`, `alternate_horizon:50day`.

**New project-wide rule added to Section 1, 2026-09-25**: every ML-track experiment comparing a richer/nonlinear model to a simpler baseline must include a `shuffled_target_placebo` check. Prompted directly by the interaction experiment (Section 44), where this was the single most decisive check in the run. **Correction, stated plainly**: a request referenced this as an already-existing "manifest Section 1 rule" before this update — it did not yet exist in CLAUDE.md at that point; it's added here, now, for the first time, not retroactively claimed as pre-existing.

**Placeholder note**: `train_period`/`validation_period`/`test_period` literal boundaries are a first-pass 60/20/20 split of the confirmed window, pending the actual joined observation count — same disclosed-placeholder discipline as every other experiment in this family, re-verify once real N is known.

**Data-generation pass complete, 2026-09-25 — real dataset built, verified, Stage 1 still NOT run.** New script `generate_xgboost_volatility_dataset.py`, using the full paginated fetch (`gex_data_loader.py:_fetch_raw_chain_paginated`) for every single ticker-day from the start — the pre-registration's own feasibility check had used single-page queries and found NVDA/MSTR showing 0 near-bucket expirations at one date, explicitly flagged there as a likely single-page artifact requiring the full method for the real run. Smoke-tested against that exact NVDA/MSTR/2024-06-03 case first: with full pagination (3-4 pages, 2,352-3,398 raw records vs. the earlier 1,000-record single-page cap), both buckets were genuinely populated — confirming the artifact diagnosis before committing to the full run. Saved to a **brand-new** database, `xgboost_volatility_dataset.db` (`volatility_training_examples` + `volatility_excluded` + `bucket_population_summary` tables) — no existing dataset touched.

**One real bug hit and fixed mid-run, not glossed over.** A raw EODHD record can carry `volatility=0.0` (not `None`) — degenerate data that crashed `calculate_delta()` with a `ZeroDivisionError` (divides by `volatility × sqrt(t)`). The crash hit partway through AAPL, after 631/678 days were already safely committed. **Fixed**: `select_near_far()` now requires strictly positive IV, not just "not None." Resumed cleanly from the saved 631 days. **Concurrency-safety note**: this script includes a new exclusive-lockfile mechanism (`xgboost_volatility_generation.lock`, `os.O_CREAT|O_EXCL`) added specifically because the background-execution tooling had twice silently double-launched a script earlier this session (Section 44) — confirmed working this run: exactly one process ran at a time across both the crashed run and the resume, verified via `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process` and a clean single `RUN START`/`RUN COMPLETE` pair in the log for the successful run.

**Per-ticker near/far DTE bucket population — verified directly from the DB, not trusted from the script's own printed summary (which undercounted AAPL due to the resume splitting its processing across two runs — a real reporting artifact, caught and corrected before reporting final numbers):**

| Ticker | Total candidates | Saved | Excluded | Near-bucket populated | Far-bucket populated |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| AAPL | 678 | 667 | 11 | 667/678 (98.4%) | 667/678 (98.4%) |
| TSLA | 678 | 672 | 6 | 674/678 (99.4%) | 674/678 (99.4%) |
| **NVDA** | 678 | 672 | 6 | **672/678 (99.1%)** | **672/678 (99.1%)** |
| AMD | 678 | 673 | 5 | 673/678 (99.3%) | 675/678 (99.6%) |
| SMCI | 678 | 672 | 6 | 673/678 (99.3%) | 673/678 (99.3%) |
| **MSTR** | 678 | 671 | 7 | **673/678 (99.3%)** | **672/678 (99.1%)** |
| **Aggregate** | 4,068 | **4,027** | **41** | 4,032/4,068 (99.1%) | 4,033/4,068 (99.1%) |

**NVDA and MSTR — the two tickers the pre-registration's single-page feasibility check had wrongly flagged as potentially broken — now sit at 99.1-99.3%, statistically indistinguishable from the other four tickers.** No ticker shows a degenerate or suspiciously low bucket-population rate anywhere in the full ~3-year window; the single-page artifact is confirmed fully resolved, not just at the one originally-flagged date. All 41 exclusions (37 `term_structure_unavailable`, 4 `oi_skew_unavailable`) are legitimate, individually logged, never-substituted data gaps.

**Final dataset: 4,027 rows**, one observation per (ticker, trading day), 8 features (`term_structure_slope_pct`, `oi_skew`, `trailing_realized_vol_20d`, `delta`, `gamma`, `theta`, `charm`, `vega_per_1pct_iv`) + 3 forward realized-volatility target horizons (10/20/50-day) each, spanning 2023-10-02 to 2026-09-24, all 6 tickers.

---

### Stage 1 EXECUTED, 2026-09-25 — REJECTED, but with a genuinely mixed, non-obvious result underneath, not a clean signal failure.

**Setup**: 4,027 rows, chronological split train=2,417 (2023-10-30 to 2025-06-16)/val=809/test=801 (by real day-count, not the placeholder literals). **Primary (locked, thinned) test set: 174 observations** — clears N≥100. Model A = Ridge (tuned alpha=100.0 via nested CV, MAE-based). Model B = XGBoost (tuned max_depth=3, min_child_weight=5, reg_alpha=1.0, reg_lambda=1.0, subsample=0.8, colsample_bytree=0.8). Features standardized (fit on train+val) before both models, so both see literally identical scaled inputs.

**Primary result — MAE and R² disagree, a real pattern, not noise:**

| Seed | R² A | R² B | R² delta | MAE A | MAE B | Wilcoxon p |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 42 | −0.327 | −0.744 | **−0.417** | 0.2799 | 0.2551 | **2.15×10⁻⁶** |
| 43 | −0.327 | −0.355 | −0.028 | 0.2799 | 0.2491 | **1.06×10⁻⁴** |

Wilcoxon on absolute error is overwhelmingly significant at both seeds (XGBoost's typical prediction is measurably closer), but **R² is negative for both models and worse for XGBoost** — meaning XGBoost does better on typical days but makes a small number of much larger misses that squared-error-based R² punishes heavily. Both models' negative R² means neither beats "predict the mean" in aggregate. Effect-size gate (R² delta ≥ +0.05, both seeds) fails, and fails negatively, not marginally.

**5 robustness checks, full texture, not rounded to a blanket pass/fail:**
1. **`alternate_model:random_forest` — does NOT corroborate XGBoost; points the opposite direction.** RF: R²=**+0.0708** (genuinely positive, clears "predict the mean"), delta vs. Ridge=**+0.398**, p=2.0×10⁻⁷. A real disagreement between the two tree-based model classes — RF looks like a real winner here where XGBoost does not.
2. **`leave_one_ticker_out` — wildly heterogeneous, a real concentration finding.** Excluding AMD or SMCI flips XGBoost's delta strongly positive (+0.481, +0.377, both highly significant); excluding AAPL/MSTR/NVDA/TSLA instead makes it worse (−0.55 to −1.42). **AMD and SMCI specifically are what drag the full-population R² deeply negative.**
3. **`alternate_horizon:10day`**: delta ≈ 0 (+0.0008), p=0.0003 — same MAE/R² divergence pattern, negligible effect size.
4. **`alternate_horizon:50day`**: delta **+0.642**, p=2.2×10⁻⁸ — XGBoost clearly wins at this horizon.
5. **`shuffled_target_placebo` — clean.** Delta on shuffled labels: −0.265, p=0.983 (not significant). No leakage — the pipeline is sound.

**Concentration check** matches leave-one-ticker-out directly: AMD (−0.338) and MSTR (−0.095) show XGBoost underperforming Ridge on mean per-ticker absolute-error difference; AAPL (+0.227), SMCI (+0.164), TSLA (+0.138), NVDA (+0.053) show XGBoost ahead. Genuinely mixed, not uniformly noise-like.

**Verdict: REJECTED**, on two literal triggers — effect size below 0.05 at both seeds, and `leave_one_ticker_out` reversing direction. **Failure classification: insufficient evidence / genuinely mixed, NOT a clean genuine-signal-failure** (contrast with the win/loss XGBoost interaction experiment, Section 44, which was a clean rejection). The primary Wilcoxon result is strongly significant, Random Forest clears every bar on the identical data/features, the 50-day horizon clearly favors XGBoost, and the effect concentrates in exactly two tickers rather than looking like noise across the board. Reads more like "XGBoost specifically has an outlier/stability problem on this feature set that Random Forest doesn't share" than "there's no learnable structure here" — but the pre-registration's literal decision rules, locked before this result existed, say REJECTED as specified, and that verdict stands without relitigating the gate after seeing an interesting result.

**Updated running tally: five closed hypotheses (V1, GEX call-wall-distance, GEX net gamma regime, XGBoost interactions, XGBoost volatility-vs-Ridge), one SUGGESTIVE-pending-Stage-2 (IV term structure), two scoped-not-run (strike positioning, SPY GEX/volatility).**

**Status: fully executed, documented, closed.** A natural, undecided future direction (not started, not committed to): a freshly-designed, separately-pre-registered hypothesis specifically testing Random Forest vs. Ridge on this same feature/target combination — since RF (not XGBoost) is what actually cleared every bar here — would be a genuinely new, honestly-motivated hypothesis, not a retune of this one.

---

## 46. Eighth formal experiment: Random Forest vs. Ridge on forward realized volatility — CLOSED: REJECTED at Stage 1 (2026-09-25)

**Directly motivated by a robustness-check finding in the closed/REJECTED `xgboost_volatility_prediction_v1_20260925` (Section 45), NOT a retune of it.** `preregister_randomforest_volatility_experiment.py`. Same discipline as every pre-registration in this project: constructs a real `ExperimentConfig`, no path to `preregister_experiment()`/`run_experiment()`, verified to construct cleanly. **`config_hash`: `0655c4a23b41024954b07f29fb5d813b873fd14d9dd145f17640a3549af4557e`.**

**Why**: the closed XGBoost-volatility experiment's own `alternate_model:random_forest` robustness check showed Random Forest — not XGBoost — clearing every VALIDATED bar on the identical data (R²=+0.0708, effect delta vs. Ridge=+0.398, p=2.0×10⁻⁷), and its `leave_one_ticker_out` check showed XGBoost's aggregate failure was concentrated specifically in AMD and SMCI. **Mechanism, stated explicitly, not assumed**: bagging (Random Forest, independent bootstrap-resampled trees) is structurally more robust to a subgroup dominating the fit than boosting (XGBoost, sequential residual correction) is.

**Honest methodological caveat, stated up front, not glossed over**: this hypothesis is **not fully blind** — it's directly informed by a robustness-check finding from a closed, model-class-distinct experiment on the same data. That's a real, disclosed limitation on how much independent confirming weight a positive result should carry. It does not make the result predetermined — Random Forest could show the same AMD/SMCI instability. **`leave_one_ticker_out` is declared mandatory specifically because of this**, so a repeat of the AMD/SMCI pattern can't be treated as a surprise requiring a new check invented after the fact — it's pre-specified now that a reversal there counts as a genuine robustness failure.

**Zero new data collection**: reuses `xgboost_volatility_dataset.db`'s existing, verified 4,027-row dataset exactly as-is — same 8 features, same 3 horizons, same universe. Model A/primary test/effect-size floor/Bonferroni family/min-N/seeds all reused verbatim from the closed experiment; only Model B (XGBoost → Random Forest) and the alternate-model robustness check (Random Forest → XGBoost, closing the loop) changed. Train/val/test period boundaries are the **real, already-known** values from the closed experiment's own executed split on this identical dataset (2023-10-02/2025-06-16/2025-12-30/2026-07-15) — not re-derived placeholders, since re-splitting the same 4,027 rows the same way necessarily reproduces the same boundary.

**Stage 2 was designed** (genuinely rolling, fixed start 2026-07-16, open end via data-existence check only, N≥100 lock-and-test-once — same discipline as IV term structure's Stage 2, Section 33) **but never populated or run — Stage 1's own REJECTED verdict (below) closed the experiment before Stage 2 was needed, per this project's established precedent (a REJECTED Stage 1 makes Stage 2 moot, same as GEX call-wall-distance/net-gamma-regime, Sections 32/42).** Left here only as a record of the design work, not as a live/pending plan.

---

### Stage 1 EXECUTED, 2026-09-25 — REJECTED per the literal decision rules, triggered by a single check whose disagreement the hypothesis itself predicted; every other criterion passed cleanly and strongly.

**Setup verified, not assumed**: split boundaries sanity-checked programmatically against the pre-registration's cited real values (train ends 2025-06-16, val 2025-12-30, test 2026-07-15) — exact match. Primary (locked, thinned) test set: 174 observations. Model A = Ridge (tuned alpha=100.0). Model B = Random Forest (tuned max_depth=4, min_samples_leaf=10, max_features='sqrt').

**Primary result — passes cleanly at both seeds:**

| Seed | R² A | R² B | R² delta | MAE A | MAE B | Wilcoxon p |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 42 | −0.327 | **+0.0708** | **+0.398** | 0.2799 | 0.2296 | **2.0×10⁻⁷** |
| 43 | −0.327 | +0.0312 | +0.359 | 0.2799 | 0.2308 | **7.5×10⁻⁸** |

Random Forest clears "predict the mean" (positive R²) at both seeds — Ridge and (per the closed experiment) XGBoost both do not. Effect size clears 0.05 by ~7x; both p-values many orders of magnitude below the corrected α=0.0167.

**5 robustness checks, individually:**
1. **`alternate_model:xgboost` — FAILS, the sole rejection trigger.** R²=−0.744, delta vs. Ridge=**−0.417**, p=2.15×10⁻⁶ — reproduces XGBoost's own primary result from the closed experiment exactly (same model, same data, deterministic). **This is precisely what the hypothesis predicted** (bagging vs. boosting behave differently on this ticker-heterogeneous data) — a real, disclosed design tension in this pre-registration's own decision rules, flagged explicitly before running: requiring the other tree model to ALSO show a positive effect, when the whole point of the hypothesis is that the two should differ, is an awkward fit for this specific check. Not reinterpreted after seeing the result — the literal rule says REJECTED, so it is.
2. **`leave_one_ticker_out` — the check that mattered most, and it came back clean.** All 6 exclusions positive, **no AMD/SMCI reversal** (the exact pattern this experiment was built to test for): excl AAPL +0.312 (p=0.0008), excl AMD **+0.671** (p=1.3×10⁻¹⁵, stronger without AMD but never reversing), excl MSTR +0.200 (p=2.2×10⁻⁶), excl NVDA +0.110 (p=0.0021), excl SMCI +0.372 (p=2.4×10⁻⁶), excl TSLA +0.238 (p=1.9×10⁻⁵). Directly confirms the stated bagging-vs-boosting mechanism.
3. **`alternate_horizon:10day`**: delta **+0.354**, p=1.3×10⁻¹¹.
4. **`alternate_horizon:50day`**: delta **+0.942**, p=1.5×10⁻¹² — the strongest result in the run.
5. **`shuffled_target_placebo` — clean.** Delta on shuffled labels: −0.071, p=0.967 (not significant). No leakage.

**Concentration check**: AMD (−0.135) and MSTR (−0.039) show RF marginally behind Ridge per-ticker; AAPL (+0.131), SMCI (+0.205), TSLA (+0.112), NVDA (+0.027) show RF ahead — mixed, no single ticker dominates the way XGBoost's AMD/SMCI pattern did.

**Stage 1 verdict: REJECTED**, triggered solely by `alternate_model:xgboost`.

**CORRECTED 2026-09-25, same day — an initial draft of this section wrongly stated "Overall status: still INCONCLUSIVE regardless of this Stage 1 outcome," treating the result as held-open pending Stage 2. That was wrong and has been fixed, not softened.** Checked the pre-registration's own literal `DECISION_RULES` text directly (not from memory): the REJECTED clause is a flat OR — *"Primary N >= 100 but the primary test is not significant... OR effect size is below 0.05, OR direction reverses under any of the first four robustness checks, OR shuffled_target_placebo DOES show a significant effect"* — with no carve-out anywhere for an isolated single-check failure alongside an otherwise-strong result. The file's own INCONCLUSIVE clause is explicitly scoped to *"Stage 1 passes but Stage 2 has not yet reached N>=100"* — it has no defined path for a REJECTED Stage 1. Per this project's own established precedent (GEX call-wall-distance and GEX net-gamma-regime, Sections 32/42): a REJECTED Stage 1 closes the experiment under its own pre-specified rules; Stage 2 replication is moot for a hypothesis that already failed on historical data.

**This experiment is REJECTED AND CLOSED — Stage 2 is moot, not pending.** No exception made for how strong the rest of the numbers looked (strongly significant primary result at both seeds, effect size ~7x the threshold, a clean leave-one-ticker-out with zero AMD/SMCI repeat, both alternate horizons strongly positive, a clean leakage placebo) — the pre-registration's literal rule doesn't have a "but everything else was good" exception, and none is invented after the fact. **Worth recording precisely, not as a hedge**: the substance here is genuinely different in character from the other REJECTED experiments in this project (this one failed on a single check whose disagreement its own stated mechanism predicted, not on a weak/noise-level primary result) — but that observation does not change the verdict itself, only how informative a future, freshly-designed follow-up hypothesis might be if someone chooses to build one later (a new pre-registration, not a reopening of this one).

**Updated running tally: seven closed hypotheses (V1, GEX call-wall-distance, GEX net gamma regime, XGBoost interactions, XGBoost volatility-vs-Ridge, Random-Forest volatility-vs-Ridge), one SUGGESTIVE-pending-Stage-2 (IV term structure), two scoped-not-run (strike positioning, SPY GEX/volatility).**

**Status: fully executed, documented, CLOSED. No Stage 2, no further action** on this specific hypothesis.

## 47. Diagnostic investigation: why did Random Forest and XGBoost disagree on the forward-realized-volatility dataset? (2026-09-25)

**NOT a pre-registered experiment — no config hash, no Stage 1/Stage 2, no VALIDATED/REJECTED decision rule.** Purpose was understanding model behavior after the closed `randomforest_volatility_prediction_v1_20260925` (Section 46) showed Random Forest clearing every bar while its own `alternate_model:xgboost` check showed XGBoost reversed (delta −0.417) — not claiming or testing a tradeable signal. `investigate_rf_xgboost_disagreement.py`. Reused the existing 4,027-row dataset and the same 8 features/target, zero new data collection; reused the already-found best hyperparameters for Ridge/RF/XGBoost from the two closed experiments as the baseline.

**Result: a clear, coherent explanation — not an "inconclusive" outcome.**

1. **This is overfitting, and it's not subtle.** Train-vs-test R² gap: Ridge 0.476, **Random Forest 0.326**, **XGBoost 1.589** — XGBoost fits training data nearly 2x better than RF (R²=0.678 vs. 0.373) but performs catastrophically worse out of sample (R²=−0.912 vs. +0.048).
2. **Not a seed-instability artifact — XGBoost is *consistently* bad.** All 7 tested seeds (42-48) gave negative R² (range −0.91 to −0.56, mean −0.71); Random Forest stayed mostly positive (range −0.006 to +0.072). All 6 nearby hyperparameter variants tested (shallower trees, more/less L1/L2, different subsampling) still landed XGBoost between −0.28 and −0.91 — never positive. This is a stably-wrong result, not a flip-prone one — a more informative finding than instability would have been.
3. **Permutation importance reveals the mechanism.** XGBoost shows severe *negative* permutation importance for `vega_per_1pct_iv` (−0.657) and `theta` (−0.436) — removing either feature *improves* its test R² by 0.44-0.66, a direct fingerprint of having learned a harmful, noise-fitting reliance on those two Greeks. Random Forest's importances stay modest throughout (nothing beyond ±0.25); its top feature by far is `trailing_realized_vol_20d` (+0.251), a sensible, literature-grounded predictor. Native impurity/gain importances look similar between the models — only permutation importance on the shared test set exposed this divergence.
4. **The damage concentrates in high-volatility days and the most recent test quarter, not uniformly.** XGBoost is actually *more* accurate than RF on low-vol days (error 0.108 vs. 0.175) but much worse on high-vol days (0.507 vs. 0.401) and the test period's most recent quarter (2026-05-27 to 07-15: 0.327 vs. 0.232; the three earlier quarters are comparable between models). Consistent with an overfit model extrapolating poorly exactly where training-period patterns don't generalize.
5. **Per-ticker story is more nuanced than "AMD/SMCI vs. everyone."** AMD shows severe XGBoost damage (error 0.517 vs. RF's 0.298, RF more accurate 81% of days) and likely dominates the aggregate; MSTR/SMCI show smaller RF advantages. But **AAPL shows the opposite pattern** (XGBoost error 0.069 vs. RF's 0.162, XGBoost more accurate 86% of days), and NVDA/TSLA modestly favor XGBoost too. Not a clean "two bad tickers, four neutral" story — closer to "one severely bad ticker (AMD) outweighing a genuinely mixed picture elsewhere."

**Synthesis**: XGBoost's underperformance on this dataset is well-explained by overfitting — confirmed via train-test gap, seed-robust across 7 seeds, hyperparameter-robust across 6 variants, with a traceable mechanism (harmful reliance on vega/theta) and a traceable failure pattern (high-vol spikes, the most recent test quarter, concentrated heavily in AMD). This is understanding, not validation — it does not reopen the closed Random-Forest-vs-Ridge experiment (Section 46) or establish anything about live trading use.

---

## 48. Ninth formal experiment: Random Forest vs. Ridge on forward realized volatility, v2 — pre-registration drafted (2026-09-25, NOT YET SUBMITTED, NOT RUN)

**NOT a reopening or retune of the closed/REJECTED `randomforest_volatility_prediction_v1_20260925` (Section 46) — that stays exactly as it is, closed.** `preregister_randomforest_volatility_v2_experiment.py`, `experiment_id="randomforest_volatility_prediction_v2_20260925"`. Same discipline as every pre-registration: constructs a real `ExperimentConfig`, no path to `preregister_experiment()`/`run_experiment()`, verified to construct cleanly. **`config_hash`: `472bf091313098dadbb89b28a6ff039503f7232c1e34e7b7cfee497d563b1fc5`.**

**Why a v2**: v1's sole rejection trigger was its `alternate_model:xgboost` check disagreeing. The diagnostic investigation (Section 47) directly explained that disagreement as XGBoost's own overfitting (train-test R² gap 1.589 vs. RF's 0.326, consistent across 7 seeds and 6 hyperparameter variants, traced to harmful vega/theta reliance) — not a genuine contradiction of Random Forest's result. **Dropped**: `alternate_model:xgboost` as a robustness gate — stated explicitly in the file: requiring RF to agree with a model independently shown to be overfitting tests XGBoost's stability, not RF's validity.

**Doubly-informed disclosure, extending v1's own "not fully blind" caveat**: this hypothesis is informed twice over — first by v1's own result, second by the diagnostic that explained v1's rejection. An eventual positive result here should be read with that fuller lineage in mind, not treated as an independent, low-information confirmation.

**New/enhanced robustness checks, each motivated by a specific diagnostic finding, replacing the dropped one:**
1. **`leave_one_ticker_out`, enhanced** — AMD's own exclusion result must be reported as its own explicitly-labeled figure, not folded into an unlabeled average (AMD was the single most XGBoost-damaged ticker in the diagnostic and has now been destabilizing in two separate contexts, here and Section 44).
2. **`stability_across_seeds_and_hyperparameters`** (new) — RF's *own* R² delta vs. Ridge must stay positive across all 7 tested seeds (42-48) AND all 12 combinations of the already-established RF hyperparameter grid — a comprehensive sweep, not just the two-seed confirmation v1 used. Explicitly disclosed nuance: RF's raw (not delta) R² dipped slightly negative at one seed in the diagnostic's exploratory pass; this check formally re-verifies the delta, the actually hypothesis-relevant quantity, rather than assuming the diagnostic already settled it.
3. **`overfitting_diagnostic_train_test_gap`** (new) — RF's train-vs-test R² gap must not exceed Ridge's own gap on the same split, computed fresh at execution time — a concrete, self-referential threshold directly targeting the exact mechanism (overfitting) the diagnostic identified as the actual problem.
4. **`shuffled_target_placebo`** — kept, mandatory, unchanged (CLAUDE.md Section 1's rule).
5. **`alternate_horizon:10day` / `alternate_horizon:50day`** — kept unchanged; never implicated by the diagnostic, so not touched. Declared_robustness_checks is therefore **6 checks, not 5** — a deliberate scope decision (only what was shown broken was removed), disclosed explicitly rather than silently padded or trimmed to a round number.

**Everything else reused unmodified from v1** — same 4,027-row dataset (zero new data collection), same 8 features/target, same real train/val/test boundaries (2023-10-02/2025-06-16/2025-12-30/2026-07-15), same effect-size floor (R² delta ≥0.05), same primary test (paired Wilcoxon on absolute error, Bonferroni family=3, α_corrected=0.0167), same Stage 2 plan (rolling, fixed start 2026-07-16, N≥100, currently zero rows, shared/identical state with v1's own Stage 2 definition since neither was ever populated).

**REJECTED clause carries the same no-exception standard as v1 and every other closure in this project** — stated explicitly in the decision rules: any of the 6 declared checks failing closes the experiment REJECTED, no isolated-failure carve-out for an otherwise-strong result.

---

### Stage 1 EXECUTED, 2026-09-25 — VALIDATED. Every criterion met at both seeds, all 6 robustness checks passed with no exceptions needed. Overall status still gated on Stage 2 (zero rows, not yet run).

**Setup verified**: split boundaries sanity-checked programmatically against the pre-registration's cited real values — exact match. Primary (locked, thinned) test set: 174 observations. Single clean process run confirmed (no duplicate-launch recurrence).

**Primary result:**

| Seed | R² A (Ridge) | R² B (RF) | R² delta | Wilcoxon p |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 42 | −0.327 | +0.0708 | **+0.398** | 2.02×10⁻⁷ |
| 43 | −0.327 | +0.0312 | +0.359 | 7.48×10⁻⁸ |

**All 6 declared checks, individually, none rounded up:**
1. **`leave_one_ticker_out`, AMD reported explicitly**: all 6 exclusions positive. **AMD's own figure: delta=+0.671, p=1.29×10⁻¹⁵** — no instability; excluding AMD makes the result stronger, but it never reverses.
2. **`stability_across_seeds_and_hyperparameters` — PASS, comprehensively.** All 7 seeds (42-48) positive delta (range +0.341 to +0.421). All 12 RF hyperparameter grid combinations positive delta (range +0.250 to +0.398) — even where RF's raw R² dipped negative under `max_features='log2'` variants, its delta vs. Ridge stayed positive throughout. 19/19 clean.
3. **`overfitting_diagnostic_train_test_gap` — PASS.** RF gap=0.326 (R² train 0.373 → test 0.048) vs. Ridge gap=0.476 — RF overfits *less* than the linear baseline.
4. **`alternate_horizon:10day`**: delta +0.354, p=1.3×10⁻¹¹.
5. **`alternate_horizon:50day`**: delta +0.942, p=1.5×10⁻¹².
6. **`shuffled_target_placebo` — clean.** Delta on shuffled labels: −0.071, p=0.967. No leakage.

**Concentration check, one nuance disclosed, not glossed over**: this per-ticker MAE-based check (a different computation from leave-one-ticker-out — evaluates the full model's predictions, not a refit) shows AMD as the one ticker where Ridge slightly edges RF on raw MAE (−0.135), even though AMD's own LOTO figure was strongly positive. Different metrics telling a slightly different local story, not a contradiction — AMD remains the most idiosyncratic ticker in this dataset for RF, just not to a degree that breaks any pre-specified check.

**Stage 1 verdict: VALIDATED.** Every criterion met at both seeds, all 6 robustness checks passed cleanly — no exceptions invoked, no isolated failure to explain away, unlike v1. **Overall status remains gated on Stage 2** (rolling window, fixed start 2026-07-16, currently zero rows, shared/identical state with v1's own never-populated Stage 2) — per the decision rules, this is Stage-1-VALIDATED, not overall-VALIDATED, until Stage 2 independently replicates once N≥100 is reached (estimated weeks, per the pre-registration's own disclosed estimate).

**Updated running tally: seven closed hypotheses (V1, GEX call-wall-distance, GEX net gamma regime, XGBoost interactions, XGBoost volatility-vs-Ridge, Random-Forest volatility-vs-Ridge v1), one Stage-1-VALIDATED-pending-Stage-2 (Random-Forest volatility-vs-Ridge v2 — this is the FIRST hypothesis in this entire project's ML track to clear every Stage 1 bar cleanly), one SUGGESTIVE-pending-Stage-2 (IV term structure), two scoped-not-run (strike positioning, SPY GEX/volatility).**

**Status: Stage 1 fully executed and documented. Stage 2 not yet run** (zero rows as of 2026-09-25; re-check the data-existence query periodically, estimated weeks not months). Per this project's own philosophy (Section 1): a clean Stage-1 pass is a real, positive milestone, but not implementation-ready — genuine out-of-sample Stage 2 replication is still required before any elevated confidence beyond "Stage 1 VALIDATED, awaiting confirmation."

## 49. Diagnostic investigation: European vs. American option pricing — is this project's pricing chain missing a real early-exercise adjustment? (2026-09-25)

**NOT a pre-registered experiment — no config hash, no VALIDATED/REJECTED decision rule**, same category as the diagnostic in Section 47. A correctness question about existing infrastructure, not a new trading signal. `investigate_american_vs_european_pricing.py`.

**Step 1, confirmed empirically, not assumed**: `options_pricing.py:black_scholes_price()` — the single pricing function every one of 78+ files in this project's V1/V2 chain, live bots, and ML feature sets calls (directly or via `options_backtest_engine.py`'s re-export) — is plain closed-form European Black-Scholes. No early-exercise boundary, no dividend term at all. Grepped the entire codebase for `binomial`/`barone`/`adesi`/`bjerksund`/`whaley`/`longstaff`/`early_exercise`: **zero hits anywhere.**

**Step 2, quantified via a from-scratch Longstaff-Schwartz (LSM) American pricer, applied to the real 2,599-row trade population** (`ml_xgboost_interaction_data.db`, real tickers/entry dates/strikes/EODHD historical IV — the same validated population Section 44/45 built, zero new data collection, DTE confirmed constant at 14 across all rows).

**A real bug in the investigation itself was found and fixed before trusting any result — disclosed, not hidden.** The first pass (8,000 Monte Carlo paths/contract) showed a mean gap of +0.62% with outliers to ±9% — too large to trust against the rigorous theoretical fact that an American call on a non-dividend underlying must price *exactly* the same as the European call. A targeted convergence check (4 real trades at 8k/40k/150k/400k paths) confirmed a **systematic +2.4-2.9% upward bias at 8,000 paths** — the well-known LSM in-sample regression bias (same path set fits the continuation-value regression and prices the option) — shrinking at higher path counts. Discarded the 8,000-path run; reran the full population at 150,000 paths.

**Corrected result, with an honest limit stated on what can be claimed from it:**

| | Zero-dividend (matches this project's own assumption) | Realistic dividend (external estimate, disclosed — this project has no dividend data anywhere) |
|---|---|---|
| Mean gap | −0.279% | −0.369% |
| Median | −0.297% | −0.374% |
| Std | 0.542% | 0.601% |
| Range | −2.30% to +1.94% | −2.53% to +1.90% |

**Not reported as a clean measurement of a real early-exercise premium — the residual bias in the pricer itself is still visible and hadn't fully converged even at 150,000 paths** (the 4-trade convergence check showed it still shrinking, roughly −0.7% at 150k toward −0.3% at 400k). More tellingly: adding AAPL's real ~0.45%/year dividend moved its own gap from −0.41% to **−0.97% — the wrong direction** (a correctly-captured dividend effect should push American value *up* relative to European, not further down). That sign behavior is strong internal evidence the measured numbers reflect the pricer's own residual noise floor, not a genuine, correctly-signed market effect.

**Conclusion: "no real gap, Black-Scholes was fine" — not because the simulation was too weak to detect anything, but because the theory is airtight for this specific population and the empirical exercise doesn't contradict it.** 5 of 6 tickers (TSLA/AMD/SMCI/MSTR historically, NVDA negligibly) pay no meaningful dividend — early exercise of a call is never optimal without one, a rigorous, universally-accepted result, not something requiring simulation to discover. For AAPL specifically, the expected dividend captured over V1's 14-day window is ≈0.45%/year × (14/365) ≈ 0.017% of spot — smaller than a single price tick, well below this project's own already-modeled 2-12% spread-cost tiers (Section 3/15).

**No downstream results are flagged as affected** — V1, the V2 correction chain, and every ML feature set built on these prices are unaffected by the missing American-exercise logic, since the theoretically-expected true gap for this calls-only, near-non-dividend population is negligible. Per explicit instruction, this closes the correctness question without needing a fix and does not retroactively reinterpret any closed experiment's verdict.

---

## 51. Tenth formal experiment: heavily-regularized XGBoost vs. Ridge on forward realized volatility — pre-registration drafted (2026-09-25, NOT YET SUBMITTED, NOT RUN)

**NOT a reopening of the closed/REJECTED `xgboost_volatility_prediction_v1_20260925` (Section 45)** — that stays exactly as it is. `preregister_xgboost_volatility_v2_experiment.py`, `experiment_id="xgboost_volatility_prediction_regularized_v2_20260925"`. **Hash history: `0276337ca56cced3fa58fe92cb2cbc4b312b1186b39d42493e5530ea382ba1d2` (original draft, 2026-09-25) → `1bccd0ba4b8b6f9350bde3f1c7dba97b93a48db9cfa679afecb2b3605ffdab97` (current, 2026-09-26 — gap-closure criterion added, see below).**

**CORRECTED/AMENDED 2026-09-26, before Stage 1 execution — a real, disclosed gap, not silently patched.** A pre-Stage-1 review asked whether the file pinned an explicit, pre-committed threshold for "regularization closes the gap to Random Forest," rather than just "beats unregularized XGBoost" or "beats Ridge." Checked directly: it did not — `effect_size_threshold_r2_delta=0.05` was only ever a beats-Ridge floor (identical to v1's), and the RF v2 comparison was explicitly declared "descriptive only, NOT a decision-rule gate." **Added, before any Stage 1 result existed**: a new `gap_closure_criterion`, requiring the regularized XGBoost's R² delta vs. Ridge to *also* clear ≥50% of Random Forest v2's own already-confirmed R² delta on the identical primary test set, matched per seed using RF v2's fixed, closed-experiment reference values (seed42: ≥0.1991 = 0.50×0.3982; seed43: ≥0.1793 = 0.50×0.3586). This is additive to, not a replacement for, the existing 0.05 beats-Ridge floor — both must clear. Explicitly not the same design flaw as v1's own rejected "must agree with a different model class" gate: that gated on *direction* against an unrelated model (XGBoost vs. XGBoost); this gates on *magnitude* against RF v2's own already-locked, closed result on the same data — a legitimate, pre-committed bar for the specific "does regularization close most of the gap" question this experiment was built to answer.

**A factual correction made before designing anything, not silently accepted**: the request that prompted this draft assumed v1 used deep/unconstrained trees. Verified directly against v1's actual executed hyperparameters (Section 46's reused `alternate_model:xgboost` params): v1 already used `max_depth∈{2,3}`, and the diagnostic investigation (Section 47) already tested `max_depth=2` specifically and found it still strongly negative (R²=−0.28). "Shallow trees" is not a novel lever here — disclosed explicitly in the file rather than repeated as a new fix.

**Genuinely new levers, verified via WebSearch against real, current sources before being cited (not asserted from memory)**:
- **Early stopping** — v1 never used it (fixed `n_estimators=200`). New design: `max_boost_rounds=1000`, `early_stopping_rounds=100` (the ~10%-of-ceiling convention, per [XGBoost's official parameter-tuning docs](https://xgboost.readthedocs.io/en/stable/tutorials/param_tuning.html) and [MachineLearningMastery](https://machinelearningmastery.com/avoid-overfitting-by-early-stopping-with-xgboost-in-python/)/[mljar.com](https://mljar.com/blog/xgboost-early-stopping/)), monitored on a genuine held-out slice within each nested-CV fold.
- **Lower `learning_rate`**: {0.01, 0.02} — v1 fixed this at 0.05, never tuned.
- **Higher `reg_alpha`/`reg_lambda` ceilings**: {1.0, 5.0} / {5.0, 10.0} — v1's grid topped out at 1.0/5.0 respectively.
- **More aggressive `subsample`/`colsample_bytree` floors**: {0.6, 0.7} — v1's floor was 0.7.
- `min_child_weight` fixed at 15 (not swept, disclosed as a tractability choice, not a tuned dimension).

**Honest expectations, set before running anything, per explicit instruction — verified via WebSearch, not asserted**: cited applied-ML consensus ([GeeksforGeeks](https://www.geeksforgeeks.org/machine-learning/gradient-boosting-vs-random-forest/), [MetricGate](https://metricgate.com/blogs/random-forest-vs-gradient-boosting/)) holds that bagging (Random Forest) is structurally more resistant to noisy-data overfitting than boosting, even after regularization — an architectural difference (variance-reduction-via-averaging vs. sequential residual-correction), not just a tuning gap. **A second REJECTED verdict here, with this regularization genuinely applied, is pre-specified as legitimate, informative evidence that this dataset structurally favors Random Forest's architecture — not a reason to draft a v3 with yet more aggressive tuning.** This interpretive note doesn't alter the mechanical decision rule itself, which carries the same no-isolated-failure-carve-out standard as every other closure in this project.

**Everything else reused unmodified from v1**: same 4,027-row dataset (zero new data collection), same 8 features/target, same real Stage 1 split boundaries, same primary test (paired Wilcoxon on absolute error, R² delta ≥0.05, Bonferroni family=3, α_corrected=0.0167), same mandatory `shuffled_target_placebo`. 5 declared robustness checks: `leave_one_ticker_out` (AMD tracked explicitly, same discipline as RF v2/Section 48), `stability_across_seeds` (7 seeds, sign-consistency required, not necessarily all-positive), `alternate_horizon:10day`/`50day`, `shuffled_target_placebo`. Any comparison to RF v2's already-VALIDATED-at-Stage-1 result (Section 48) is explicitly descriptive only, not a decision-rule gate — avoiding v1's own design flaw of gating on agreement with a different model class.

**Note on citation**: the request that prompted this draft cited "Section 19a/19b/19c" — the actual current section numbers in this local CLAUDE.md are 45 (closed v1) and 47 (diagnostic investigation); flagged as likely drift against the separate, not-automatically-synced claude.ai manifest copy, not silently corrected without comment.

**Status: drafted, hashed. Stage 1 EXECUTED 2026-09-26 — REJECTED. See Section 54 for a real methodological finding (early-stopping placebo leakage) discovered and fixed mid-run, and the corrected verdict below.**

---

### Diagnostic detour, 2026-09-26 — a real pipeline bug found and fixed BEFORE trusting Stage 1's first attempt. Full writeup in Section 54; summary here for continuity.

The first Stage 1 attempt (under hash `1bccd0ba...`) showed a significant `shuffled_target_placebo` effect (p=0.0087) — per this file's own decision rules, a placebo failure voids the entire run, not just that one check. Verified via an 8-independent-shuffle-seed sweep that this was real and systematic (6/8 seeds "significant," all favoring XGBoost) — root-caused to early stopping re-deciding its own boosting-round count fresh on every fit, an adaptive step Ridge has no equivalent of. **Fixed**: the placebo check now freezes XGBoost's round count at the real-data `best_iteration` (early stopping disabled) for placebo fits only — every other fit in the script keeps full adaptive early stopping. Re-hashed: `1bccd0ba4b8b6f9350bde3f1c7dba97b93a48db9cfa679afecb2b3605ffdab97` → **`e9f7aae1458346cdc5484c138cc562b8bc01017f0317de5303e6fb0da9c57be2`** (current). The `1bccd0ba...` run's primary-test/gap-closure/robustness numbers are VOID and are not cited anywhere below. Full mechanism, evidence, and fix detail: Section 54.

### Stage 1 EXECUTED, 2026-09-26 (under the corrected hash `e9f7aae1...`) — REJECTED. Placebo now clean; regularization narrows but does not close the gap to Random Forest.

**Setup**: 4,027-row dataset, split boundaries confirmed MATCH the cited real values (train ends 2025-06-16, val 2025-12-30, test 2026-07-15). Primary (locked, thinned) test set: 174 observations. Model B tuned via the real 64-combo grid (confirmed 64/64 evaluated in the run log, correcting the file's own prior "32-combo" documentation error): winning params `max_depth=3, min_child_weight=15, reg_alpha=5.0, reg_lambda=10.0, learning_rate=0.01, subsample=0.6, colsample_bytree=0.6`, final `best_iteration=485` (well short of the 1000-round ceiling — early stopping is genuinely engaging).

**Primary result:**

| Seed | R² A (Ridge) | R² B (reg. XGB) | R² delta | Gap-closure threshold | Gap closure met? | Wilcoxon p |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 42 | −0.3273 | −0.2358 | **+0.0915** | ≥0.1991 | **No** | 2.19×10⁻⁵ |
| 43 | −0.3273 | −0.3102 | **+0.0171** | ≥0.1793 | **No** | 2.86×10⁻⁵ |

Both seeds' Wilcoxon tests are significant, but the basic beats-Ridge effect-size floor (≥0.05, both seeds) already fails at seed43 (+0.0171 < 0.05) — the gap-closure criterion is moot once the more basic bar isn't cleared. **Regularization did measurably improve XGBoost vs. v1's unregularized run** (v1's own seed42 R²=−0.744; this run's seed42 R²=−0.236) but nowhere close to Random Forest's own +0.0708/+0.0312.

**5 robustness checks, individually:**
1. **`leave_one_ticker_out` — FAILS, and the pattern is informative.** Excluding AMD: delta swings to **+0.596** (p=8.9×10⁻⁹) — the single largest number in the run. Excluding SMCI: delta **+0.397** (p=6.4×10⁻⁸). But excluding AAPL, MSTR, NVDA, or TSLA each makes the delta **negative** (−0.14 to −0.33). **AMD-specific figure, reported explicitly per the file's own discipline: r2_delta=+0.596, p=8.9×10⁻⁹** — the same ticker Section 47's diagnostic already identified as XGBoost's single worst overfitting case on this dataset is, once again, the dominant driver of the aggregate result. Regularization did not fix the AMD-specific problem; it appears to have relocated where the instability shows up (now a large single-ticker swing rather than a uniformly bad fit).
2. **`stability_across_seeds` — FAILS.** 6 of 7 seeds positive (range +0.068 to +0.202), but seed 46 is **negative** (−0.015) — sign is not consistent, exactly the kind of marginal instability the gap-closure criterion was designed to catch even where a basic Wilcoxon test still shows significance.
3. **`alternate_horizon:10day`**: delta +0.244, p=5.5×10⁻⁷ — positive, but this is a secondary check, not the primary verdict driver.
4. **`alternate_horizon:50day`**: delta +0.453, p=1.2×10⁻⁷ — same.
5. **`shuffled_target_placebo` — CLEAN, fix confirmed working.** 1 of 8 shuffle seeds significant (seed 46, p=0.0022, borderline) — within the declared ≤1/8 pass bar (chance alone predicts ~0.4/8). A dramatic contrast with the pre-fix run's 6/8 — direct, apples-to-apples confirmation the frozen-rounds carve-out resolved the real leakage mechanism, not just relabeled it.

**Concentration check**: AAPL/SMCI/TSLA favor XGBoost on mean absolute-error diff; AMD (−0.285) and MSTR (−0.049) favor Ridge — consistent with, not contradicting, the leave-one-ticker-out pattern.

**Stage 1 verdict: REJECTED** — basic effect-size floor not cleared at both seeds, gap-closure criterion consequently not met, `stability_across_seeds` sign-flips, `leave_one_ticker_out` reverses under 4 of 6 exclusions. **Per this pre-registration's own interpretive note, written before this result existed**: this is legitimate, informative evidence that heavier regularization narrows but does not close the architectural gap to Random Forest's bagging approach on this dataset — not a reason to draft a v3 with yet more aggressive XGBoost tuning.

**Updated running tally: eight closed hypotheses (V1, GEX call-wall-distance, GEX net gamma regime, XGBoost interactions, XGBoost volatility-vs-Ridge v1, Random-Forest volatility-vs-Ridge v1, regularized-XGBoost volatility-vs-Ridge v2), one Stage-1-VALIDATED-pending-Stage-2 (Random-Forest volatility-vs-Ridge v2, Section 48), one SUGGESTIVE-pending-Stage-2 (IV term structure), two scoped-not-run (strike positioning, SPY GEX/volatility).**

---

## 54. Diagnostic investigation: early-stopping placebo leakage in the regularized XGBoost v2 pipeline (2026-09-26)

**NOT a pre-registered experiment — no config hash, no VALIDATED/REJECTED decision rule**, same category as the diagnostics in Sections 47/49/50. A pipeline-correctness question surfaced mid-execution of `xgboost_volatility_prediction_regularized_v2_20260925` (Section 51), not a new trading signal.

**Trigger**: the first Stage 1 attempt at the regularized XGBoost v2 experiment (under the then-current hash `1bccd0ba...`) showed a statistically significant `shuffled_target_placebo` result (p=0.0087 at shuffle seed 42) — training on randomly-permuted labels and still showing Model B "beat" Model A on the real test set. Per this project's own rule (Section 1: every ML-track richer-vs-baseline comparison requires this check, added after Section 44's experience), a placebo failure is treated as a whole-pipeline red flag, not an isolated check to shrug off — the run's other numbers were not trusted or cited pending investigation.

**Step 1 — confirmed the anomaly was systematic, not a fluke of one permutation.** Reused the exact already-tuned hyperparameters from the flagged run and refit both models under 8 independent shuffle seeds (42, 43, 44, 45, 46, 100, 7, 999). Result: **6 of 8 seeds showed a "significant" effect** (p ranging 1.6×10⁻¹³ to 0.020) — far above the ~5% chance rate a true null would produce — and **every one of the 6 favored Model B (XGBoost), never Model A (Ridge)**. A one-directional bias this consistent across independent random permutations rules out ordinary noise; something structural was leaking.

**Step 2 — root cause, reasoned through and disclosed as such (not asserted as a documented external fact):** this pipeline's one genuinely new mechanism relative to every other model comparison already closed in this project (Sections 44-48) is **early stopping** — XGBoost re-decides its own boosting-round count fresh on every single fit, by watching a held-out slice's error curve. This is an *adaptive* step with no equivalent in Ridge, whose `alpha` is simply reused, fixed, across every fit (real-label or shuffled-label). Even with every other hyperparameter held fixed for the placebo check, XGBoost's round-count selection was free to re-optimize against whatever spurious pattern a given random shuffle happened to produce in its own held-out monitoring slice — occasionally landing on a round count that also happened to track the real primary-test target, purely by chance-correlated overfitting to that specific shuffle's noise. Ridge has no analogous per-shuffle adaptive freedom, so it could not exhibit the same effect.

**Step 3 — the fix, deliberately scoped as a carve-out, not a general change.** For the `shuffled_target_placebo` check only: XGBoost's `n_estimators` is now frozen at the real-data fit's own `best_iteration`, with early stopping disabled for that fit specifically (`fit_xgb_frozen_rounds()` in `xgboost_volatility_v2_stage1_execution.py`, distinct from `fit_xgb_early_stopping()` used everywhere else in the script). Every other fit in the experiment — the real primary model, all four other robustness checks, the gap-closure comparison — keeps early stopping fully adaptive, exactly as originally designed. This targets the specific mechanism identified in Step 2 without touching the actual hypothesis test.

**Step 4 — re-verified the fix on the same 8 seeds that exposed the problem, not a fresh/cherry-picked set.** Under the corrected pipeline (config re-hashed to `e9f7aae1...`, Section 51), the same 8 shuffle seeds now show **1 of 8 significant** (seed 46, p=0.0022, borderline) — consistent with the pass bar of ≤1/8 (pure chance at n=8 independent tests predicts ~0.4 expected "significant" results). A dramatic, apples-to-apples contrast with the pre-fix 6/8: the fix demonstrably resolved the mechanism, not merely relabeled the symptom.

**Scope of this finding**: specific to pipelines using XGBoost early stopping compared against a model with no adaptive-selection analog, evaluated via a shuffled-target placebo that reuses fixed (not re-tuned) hyperparameters across shuffles. Does not implicate any other already-closed experiment in this project — `xgboost_feature_interactions_v1_20260924` (Section 44) and `xgboost_volatility_prediction_v1_20260925` (Section 45) both used fixed `n_estimators` with no early stopping at all, so this specific leakage path was never available to them; their own clean placebo results (Section 44's 44/45's, both non-significant) stand as reported.

**Takeaway for future sessions**: if a future experiment reintroduces XGBoost early stopping (or any other per-fit adaptive model-selection step) compared against a model without an equivalent, the `shuffled_target_placebo` check must either (a) freeze that adaptive step at its real-data value for the placebo fits specifically, as done here, or (b) explicitly disclose that the placebo doesn't control for it. Don't assume a placebo pass is automatic just because it worked for this project's earlier, non-early-stopping ML experiments.

---

## 56. Data-integrity remediation: raw/unadjusted split prices confirmed contaminating `xgboost_volatility_dataset.db` — fixed, dataset regenerated, all four affected experiments rerun (2026-09-27/28)

**Trigger**: while scoping the statistical-arbitrage research design (delivered in-conversation, not yet written to this file pending sign-off on its own open decisions), the required data audit confirmed empirically that this project's entire Alpaca fetch layer requests RAW (unadjusted) prices by default (no script anywhere passes `adjustment=`). Real split discontinuities were directly verified in the live data for AAPL (2020-08-31, 4:1), NVDA (2024-06-10, 10:1), SMCI (2024-09-30, 10:1), TSLA (2022-08-25, 3:1). A follow-up question connected this to an already-documented pattern: Sections 45/47 both independently flagged AMD/SMCI as destabilizing XGBoost's forward-realized-volatility result, and Section 47's diagnostic explicitly traced the failure to "high-volatility days." NVDA and SMCI are exactly the two split-affected tickers.

**Confirmed conclusively, not just plausibly**, by querying `xgboost_volatility_dataset.db` directly: the dataset-wide maximum for both `trailing_realized_vol_20d` and `target_realized_vol_20d` (8.3356, vs. a normal ~0.3–1.0 range) was hit repeatedly by NVDA and SMCI in multi-week windows tightly bracketing their real split dates — forward-looking targets contaminated in the ~20–50 trading days *before* each split, trailing features contaminated in the ~20 trading days *after*. This reaches beyond Sections 45/47: the same `xgboost_volatility_dataset.db` also underlies Section 46/48 (Random Forest v1 REJECTED / **v2 Stage-1-VALIDATED — this project's strongest live result at the time**) and Section 51 (regularized XGBoost v2, REJECTED).

**Immediate containment**: `RF_v2_Stage2_Nightly_Update` disabled before its next scheduled fire, to stop it appending more contaminated rows while the fix was in progress.

**Fix, two attempts — the first one was itself a real, caught bug, not glossed over:**
1. **First attempt (wrong)**: switched the fetch to `Adjustment.ALL` throughout. This broke a *different* thing: EODHD's options chain reports strikes in real, historically-quoted (unadjusted) terms. Using a split-adjusted spot price against those real strikes produces a ~10x mismatch for any date before a split — confirmed empirically (SMCI 2024-06-03: adjusted close $77.03 vs. real quoted chain strikes clustering at $850–865 that day). A full regeneration attempt under this wrong fix silently exploded `oi_skew_unavailable` exclusions for exactly the split-affected tickers (SMCI 5→206).
2. **Correct fix**: `generate_xgboost_volatility_dataset.py`'s `fetch_daily_closes()` now fetches **two parallel series** — RAW (unadjusted) closes, used only for EODHD-chain matching (`spot_price` for the moneyness band, both sides of the Greeks calculation), and ADJUSTED (`Adjustment.ALL`) closes, used only for `log_returns`/realized-vol calculations (`trailing_realized_vol_20d`, `target_realized_vol_10d/20d/50d`). Date-series alignment between the two fetches is asserted, not assumed.

**Fix verified before trusting it, multiple ways**: (a) raw close on a pre-split SMCI date now correctly falls in the same range as that day's real quoted strikes; (b) the adjusted series is genuinely continuous across the split boundary (spot-checked: 40.23→41.98→41.61→40.58→42.02, no jump); (c) after full regeneration, NVDA's values around its split are now completely smooth (~0.49–0.55 throughout); (d) the dataset-wide max dropped from 8.3356 to **2.4012** — an order of magnitude; (e) the per-ticker exclusion pattern (41 total: 37 term-structure, 4 OI-skew) now matches the *original* pre-contamination numbers exactly, confirming the set of included dates is unchanged, only the corrupted values are fixed.

**One remaining elevated-but-moderate SMCI cluster investigated, not assumed to be a residual bug**: 17 dates from 2024-10-22 through 2024-12-04 still show vol readings of 1.2–2.4 (elevated vs. normal, but nowhere near the old 8.3 ceiling). Checked the date spread deliberately — these are ~3–9 weeks *after* the split, far outside any adjustment-boundary window, and line up almost exactly with SMCI's real 2024 accounting-scandal turmoil (auditor EY's resignation, Oct 30 2024; delisting-compliance concerns). Read as genuine market history, not a residual artifact.

**Contaminated dataset/log archived, not deleted**: `xgboost_volatility_dataset.CONTAMINATED_20260927.db.bak`, `xgboost_volatility_dataset.WRONG_ADJUSTED_SPOT_20260927.db.bak` (the intermediate wrong-fix attempt), plus matching `.txt.bak` logs.

### All four affected experiments rerun against the clean dataset, exact same pre-registration files/hashes, no design changes — per explicit instruction, priority order: RF v2 → RF v1 → XGBoost-vs-Ridge v1 → regularized XGBoost v2

**Minor, benign, disclosed discrepancy affecting three of the four reruns**: `randomforest_volatility_v2_stage1_execution.py` and `randomforest_volatility_stage1_execution.py` (which split chronologically over the *full* table, including Stage-2 rows) both flagged `MISMATCH -- INVESTIGATE` against their originally-cited split boundaries (train end shifted 2025-06-16→06-18, test end 2026-07-15→07-16, primary N 174→173). Root cause: identical total row count (4,033) but the percentile-based boundary is sensitive to exactly which calendar dates fall where, and a same-count-different-composition dataset can shift the 60/80% crossing by a day or two. `xgboost_volatility_v2_stage1_execution.py` (which explicitly locks `WHERE obs_date<=2026-07-15`) showed a clean `MATCH`, N=174 exactly as before — confirming this is a boundary-sensitivity artifact of the unlocked scripts, not a data problem.

| Experiment | Before (contaminated) | After (clean) | Verdict change? |
|---|---|---|---|
| **RF v2** (Section 48) | R²_A=−0.327, R²_B=+0.0708/+0.0312, delta=**+0.398/+0.359**, p≈2×10⁻⁷/7×10⁻⁸ — **VALIDATED at Stage 1**, all 6 checks clean | R²_A=+0.296, R²_B=+0.367, delta=**+0.071/+0.071**, p=0.69/0.77 — not significant; `leave_one_ticker_out` reverses (SMCI); `overfitting_diagnostic_train_test_gap` fails (RF gap 0.146 > Ridge gap 0.111) | **YES — VALIDATED → REJECTED** |
| **RF v1** (Section 46) | REJECTED, sole trigger: `alternate_model:xgboost` reversed (delta=−0.417) — later explained by Section 47's diagnostic as XGBoost overfitting on contaminated data | REJECTED, but `alternate_model:xgboost` **no longer reverses** (delta=+0.095, p=0.018) — directly resolves the original RF/XGBoost disagreement. Now fails on primary non-significance instead (same core numbers as RF v2 above) | No (stays REJECTED), but the *reason* changed materially — the original rejection trigger is resolved |
| **XGBoost-vs-Ridge v1** (Section 45) | R²_A=−0.327, R²_B=−0.744, delta=**−0.417** — decisive, catastrophic overfit | R²_A=+0.296, R²_B=+0.391, delta=**+0.095**, p=0.018/0.020 — clears the 0.05 effect floor, *narrowly* misses the 0.0167 corrected significance bar; `leave_one_ticker_out` still reverses via SMCI | No (stays REJECTED), but transformed from a decisive failure into a genuine near-miss |
| **Regularized XGBoost v2** (Section 51) | delta=+0.0915/+0.0171 — failed the basic 0.05 floor at seed43; REJECTED cleanly | delta=**+0.195/+0.199**, p<0.0001 both seeds — **clears both the significance bar and the 0.05 effect floor by a wide margin, all 6 seeds sign-consistent, all 6 leave-one-ticker-out exclusions positive.** But REJECTED anyway, on two complications flagged below, not a clean pass | Primary result transformed from failing to strongly passing, but two unresolved issues block a clean VALIDATED read |

**Two open, unresolved complications in the regularized XGBoost v2 rerun — flagged plainly, not resolved unilaterally:**
1. **The gap-closure criterion's reference constants are now stale.** `RF_V2_R2_DELTA = {42: 0.3982, 43: 0.3586}` are hardcoded in `xgboost_volatility_v2_stage1_execution.py`, copied from RF v2's *old, contaminated* Stage 1 result — which this same remediation just showed was itself an artifact (RF v2's clean delta is +0.071, not +0.398). The rerun's "REJECTED SPECIFICALLY ON GAP-CLOSURE" trigger is therefore comparing clean-data XGBoost against a no-longer-valid contaminated-data reference — **not a trustworthy result as currently computed.** Recomputing this threshold against RF v2's own clean numbers is a real, substantive design decision (the whole point of the gap-closure criterion) and hasn't been done — flagged for explicit direction, not quietly patched.
2. **The shuffled-target placebo, previously confirmed clean (1/8 significant) on the contaminated dataset, now shows 4/8 significant on the clean dataset** — back over the ≤1/8 pass bar. The earlier "fix confirmed working" (Section 54) was verified against that specific dataset's noise structure; it does not automatically transfer to a differently-distributed (cleaner, more genuinely informative) dataset, and this result was not chased further in this pass — flagged as needing its own fresh investigation, not assumed resolved.

**Interpretation, synthesizing across all four — matching the three outcomes the user named as equally informative in advance:**
- **RF v2's validated result did not survive clean data** — its apparent edge over Ridge was itself substantially inflated by the split contamination unfairly punishing Ridge's linear fit (unlearnable, unpredictable astronomical spikes hurt a global linear model far more than a tree-based model that can locally isolate outliers). Once removed, both models' absolute performance improved a lot (Ridge R² −0.33→+0.30; RF R² +0.07→+0.37), but RF's *relative* edge over Ridge collapsed from a dramatic, highly-significant +0.40 to a non-significant +0.07. This is a real, mechanistically-explained reversal, not noise.
- **The original RF-vs-XGBoost disagreement (Section 47's whole diagnostic) is now resolved, not just explained**: on clean data, XGBoost's own `alternate_model` check no longer reverses direction against RF — both models now agree in direction (each showing a modest edge over Ridge). The contamination was the actual cause of that disagreement, confirmed by removing it and watching the disagreement disappear.
- **One of the two previously-REJECTED XGBoost results (regularized v2) now shows a real, strong, statistically solid primary effect** — the clearest illustration that the contamination was actively suppressing a real signal for the regularized configuration specifically, not just adding noise. Whether this ultimately closes as VALIDATED depends entirely on resolving the two flagged complications above, not on anything already decided.
- **SMCI remains a genuinely destabilizing ticker across every experiment, even on clean data** (`leave_one_ticker_out` reverses on its exclusion in RF v2, XGBoost v1, and XGBoost v2's own AMD/SMCI-adjacent pattern persists) — this is no longer explained by the contamination (which is now fixed) and reads as a real, separate property of this specific ticker's data in this window, not yet investigated further.

**`RF_v2_Stage2_Nightly_Update` resumed** after the fetch-layer fix was independently verified (split-continuity check, exclusion-pattern match, SMCI-cluster explanation) — this verification is orthogonal to the two open complications above, which are execution-script/statistical-design issues layered on top of an already-confirmed-clean data-generation pipeline.

**Status: fetch layer fixed and verified, dataset regenerated and verified, all four reruns complete and reported. Two follow-up decisions await explicit direction**: (1) whether/how to recompute the regularized-XGBoost-v2 gap-closure threshold against RF v2's new clean reference numbers, and (2) whether to investigate the newly-elevated placebo-leak rate on the clean dataset before treating regularized XGBoost v2 as anything more than "REJECTED, pending unresolved methodology questions." Neither has been decided or actioned — reported, not resolved.

### `dataset_version` provenance bump, 2026-09-28 — new hashes for both affected pre-registrations, per the Section 1 rule this same remediation prompted

**Independent fact-check pass, same day**: a request to verify the three headline claims above (RF v2's contamination mechanism, regularized XGBoost v2's reversal, the re-emerged 4/8 placebo leak) against primary sources — not memory — CONFIRMED all three by direct query/rerun. Full writeup: `factcheck_rf_v2_xgboost_v2_20260928.md` (project root).

**That check surfaced a real question**: the placebo-leak fix (Section 54) got a new hash when it was fixed, but this contamination fix didn't. Resolved (Section 1's new rule, added 2026-09-28): a disclosed data-correctness fix requires no new hash on its own — the hash freezes the analysis plan, not external data content — but `dataset_version` being a static, non-content-addressed label is a real, separate traceability gap. Closed it, forward-looking only, not a re-test:

- Both `preregister_randomforest_volatility_v2_experiment.py` and `preregister_xgboost_volatility_v2_experiment.py`'s `dataset_version` field bumped to `xgboost_volatility_v1_20231002_present_6ticker_adjfix20260927_sha256_51ecca695bff10a3` — a generation-date-qualified label plus the first 16 hex chars of the real SHA-256 of `xgboost_volatility_dataset.db` as it stood 2026-09-27 ~22:42 UTC (full digest: `51ecca695bff10a37cd0c597c39562c267b94507affb512f138a799f1e102950`, verifiable via `Get-FileHash`).
- **New hashes, confirmed by rerunning both files fresh, unaffected by the accompanying docstring hash-history edits (verified stable after those too):**
  - RF v2: `472bf091313098dadbb89b28a6ff039503f7232c1e34e7b7cfee497d563b1fc5` → **`7fd2a1e91f946579397da8da50c317ca93b3983f7f589c19ec1a5f8c2f20e07f`**
  - Regularized XGBoost v2: `e9f7aae1458346cdc5484c138cc562b8bc01017f0317de5303e6fb0da9c57be2` → **`54f7986788cfa1d52b4dc695930754e09fbf7c7b6ebe32d96ab50ed815c4bbdd`**
- **The 2026-09-27 clean reruns reported throughout this section (Ridge/RF/XGBoost numbers, all robustness checks, both open complications) are the real, current Stage 1 results for these experiments** — they were computed under the prior hashes (`472bf091...`/`e9f7aae1...`) before this bump, and remain valid; the bump changes only the dataset's provenance label going forward, not any already-reported number. Any future rerun of either experiment should cite the new hashes (`7fd2a1e9...`/`54f79867...`).

## 3. Current best estimates of V1's real economics

**Frozen V1: exact parameters (verified 2026-09-20 by reading the actual `pricing_mode="v1"` code path in full, file:line cited).** V1 = `portfolio_backtest.py:run_portfolio_backtest()` with its own hardcoded, non-optional signal/exit logic (NOT `options_backtest_engine.py`'s own function defaults, which represent a different, more configurable space that V1 happens to sit inside one specific corner of).

| Parameter | Exact value | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Ticker universe | AAPL, TSLA, NVDA, AMD, SMCI, MSTR | caller (`combined_iv_spread_correction_v2.py:55`), not the engine |
| Date range | 2026-06-11 to 2026-09-09 | caller |
| SPY/market regime | bullish if daily close > 50-day daily MA, else bearish (neutral only as an insufficient-history fallback, <50 days) | `backtest_engine.py:348-379` |
| Entry breakout/breakdown | close > max(prior 20 bars' highs) [call] / close < min(prior 20 bars' lows) [put]. Hard `lookback=20`. | `backtest_engine.py:131-156` |
| Entry score | `entry_scorer.py`'s live 7-factor 0-100 score (relative volume 0-25, breakout magnitude 0-20, trend 0-20, momentum 0-15, vol regime 0-10, news 0-5, SPY alignment 0-5) — **confirmed the SAME scorer the live system uses, not a separate/older V1-only mechanism.** Early-exit shortcut: if relvol+breakout <5, returns immediately without computing the rest. | `entry_scorer.py:32-182` |
| Score threshold | `entry_score_threshold=20` (default) | `portfolio_backtest.py:107` |
| Direction | `fixed_direction="call"` is a **caller choice**, not an engine constraint — the engine itself supports dynamic per-ticker direction (call if bullish, put if bearish) via `fixed_direction=None`. V1's calls-only convention is deliberate methodology, not a limitation. | `portfolio_backtest.py:109, 215` |
| Target DTE | 14 (default) | `portfolio_backtest.py:107` |
| Strike selection | Delta-targeted search, -15%/+15% of spot in 0.5% steps, nearest to `target_delta` — NOT at-the-money, NOT a fixed % offset | `delta_strike_finder.py:17-52` via `portfolio_backtest.py:228` |
| Delta target | 0.30 (default) | `portfolio_backtest.py:107` |
| Pricing | Standard Black-Scholes, `risk_free_rate=0.04`, zero dividend term. Vol input = "hybrid": 20-trading-day realized vol, recomputed daily, EWMA-smoothed day-to-day (λ=0.90). | `options_pricing.py:7-33`, `options_backtest_engine.py:50-139` |
| Position sizing | `contracts = max(int(starting_capital × position_risk_pct / (entry_premium × 100)), 1)`, `position_risk_pct=0.01` (1%/trade) | `portfolio_backtest.py:107, 260-261` |
| Stop loss | 40% of option value lost (`stop_loss_pct=0.40`) | `portfolio_backtest.py:108, 168-169` |
| Reversal guard | **Part of V1's hardcoded exit chain, not optional or a later addition.** Fires at ≥40% unrealized gain AND last 2 closes both below(call)/above(put) the mean of the preceding 5 closes. | `portfolio_backtest.py:170-177` |
| Take-profit | **Does not exist in V1 at all.** `run_portfolio_backtest()` has no `take_profit_pct` parameter — deliberate, per its own inline comment: without the specific reversal pattern firing, a position at ≥40% gain "simply stays open." (`options_backtest_engine.py`'s separate 0.75 take-profit default is a different, not-V1 code path.) | `portfolio_backtest.py:178-184` |
| Expiration | `exit_reason="expired"` if `days_left<=0` at any checkpoint — the only other exit reason besides stop_loss/reversal_guard (3 total, full stop) | `portfolio_backtest.py:185-186` |
| Spread/execution | **NOT frictionless — V1 already bakes in an estimated (non-real-market) spread-cost haircut on both entry and exit**, tiered by premium: ≥$5→2%, ≥$2→4%, ≥$1→7%, <$1→12%. Applied unconditionally, not an opt-in flag. This is smaller/cruder than the real bid/ask work in the V1/V2 correction chain below, but "V1 synthetic backtest" does NOT mean zero execution cost. | `options_backtest_engine.py:12-26` (`_estimated_spread_cost_pct`), applied at `portfolio_backtest.py:194, 258` |
| Max hold / warm-up | No separate max-hold cap (holds until stop/reversal/expiry). Effective warm-up floor = **20 bars** (from the breakout lookback itself, not a separate index gate) — a prior `index>=60` gate was a real bug, already removed. | `portfolio_backtest.py:199-206` |
| Capital model, this session's runs | **Important nuance:** despite `PortfolioAccount`'s whole design purpose being shared cross-ticker capital, this session's reproduction calls `run_portfolio_backtest([ticker], ...)` once per ticker (single-element list) and sums afterward — so the frozen $20,427.64/251-trade number is **6 independent $100k single-ticker simulations, not one true 6-ticker shared-capital portfolio.** Capital constraints never bind across tickers in this number. Relevant to Section 7 item 8b (the missing-risk-controls gap) — the gap is even more fundamental than "controls aren't simulated," cross-ticker capital competition isn't even modeled in the first place for this specific reproduction. | `combined_iv_spread_correction_v2.py:60-64` |

That's now been done: -$1,874.37 does NOT appear anywhere in that DB.]** Queried `v1_v2_research_results.db` directly — it contains **exactly one saved row**, `V1_vs_V2_corrected_20260914_020211` (run 2026-09-14), and `full_v1_v2_research_experiment.py` is the only script that writes to it (confirmed by grep). Needs to be rebuilt with the id-date fix before trusting this number for anything.
- **Combined IV+spread correction, real bid/ask execution** (`combined_iv_spread_correction_v2.py`, built during the 2026-09-20 session): **-$45,477.00**, 248/251 trades matched (98.8% coverage). This is currently the most complete and most trustworthy real-execution estimate — it reuses the validated `_select_real_contract()` unchanged, fixes contract identity across entry/exit (same contract symbol both sides), and confirms 60.9% of real expirations differ from V1's synthetic 14-day target. **Spot-checked 2026-09-20** (3 AAPL trades against raw API JSON — see Section 7 item 1): extraction logic confirmed correct, but note the same-day-trade caveat there (28.3% of the population) before treating this as a clean signal read. This number is NOT saved to any DB — only ever produced by rerunning the script; confirmed it does not appear in `v1_v2_research_results.db` (that DB only has the IV-only-correction row above).
- The original naive combined correction (`combined_iv_spread_correction.py`, has the id-date bug) produced -$11,472.00 on only 105/251 trades (41.8% coverage) — superseded, kept only as a known-buggy reference point.
