## 8. Architecture map: data ingestion layer (Alpaca + EODHD)

*Phase 1 of a systematic codebase audit (2026-09-20), prompted by discovering an active live/shadow trading system (see below) that this file previously never documented. Phases 2-4 (calculators, pricing/selection logic, everything else) still to come — each phase adds a section here rather than overwriting this one.*

**MAJOR GAP CLOSED: there is an actively-running live/shadow trading system, separate from the V1/V2 backtest research this file otherwise documents.** Confirmed via file mtimes: `signal_log.db`, `uptime_log.db`, `options_signal_log.db`, `trades.db`, `options_trades.db` etc. were written as recently as 2026-09-18. The orchestrator is `options_shadow_loop.py` — an infinite `while True` loop (60s poll) that wires together `live_options_trader.py` (signal+entry), `options_exit_monitor.py` (exits), `daily_price_audit.py` (self-healing Alpaca reconciliation), `automated_uptime_monitor.py`, `account_review.py` (weekly), `uptime_log.py` (heartbeat), and `options_kill_server.py` (kill switch). Its docstring used to state it "places NO real orders -- everything is [shadow/simulated]." **[CORRECTED 2026-09-20, Phase 3b: that claim was STALE AND FALSE — see Section 11. `live_options_trader.py` has had `OPTIONS_SHADOW_MODE = False` since "first supervised test" and has been submitting real (paper) market orders; this loop's name/docstring/console output never caught up to that change. Still paper-only, no capital risk. The docstring and console output in `options_shadow_loop.py` were corrected the same day to read the actual `OPTIONS_SHADOW_MODE` flag and label output accurately — no behavior change, labels only. The FILE'S OWN NAME (`options_shadow_loop.py`) and its function names (`run_options_shadow_loop`, `run_options_shadow_cycle`) still say "shadow" and were deliberately left alone (a rename would touch every import site) — trust the flag and the corrected docstring/output, not the filename.]** A parallel, distinct system exists for `spy_gamma_daytrader.py` / `spy_gamma_shadow_loop.py` (SPY-specific gamma strategy) — audited in Phase 2/3, genuinely still shadow-only (see Section 9, Section 11).

**Paper-trading confirmed, consistently, everywhere.** Every `TradingClient` instantiation found (`options_data.py`, `daily_price_audit.py`, `main.py`, `live_trader.py`, `live_options_trader.py`) hardcodes `paper=True`. `live_trader.py` and `live_options_trader.py` both carry an explicit docstring line: "paper=True is hardcoded in TradingClient and is never configurable." No live/real-money order path exists anywhere in the audited files.

**Alpaca integration:**
- **Historical bars (canonical source):** `backtest_engine.py:get_historical_bars()` — 5-min IEX bars, in-memory-cached by `(ticker, start_utc, end_utc)`, cache never persisted to disk. Verified via grep (`from backtest_engine import|get_historical_bars\(`) as the single most-used data function in the codebase: **78 files** import or call it, including the backtest engine, the GEX pipeline (`gex_data_loader.py` uses it to find real trading days), and most diagnostic/audit scripts.
- **Duplicate, uncached bar-fetch paths exist alongside it** — a real inconsistency, not yet a confirmed bug: `options_evaluator.py` (`get_recent_volatility()`, `get_intraday_move()`) and `main.py` (line ~274) each instantiate their own `StockHistoricalDataClient` and call `get_stock_bars()` directly, bypassing `backtest_engine.py`'s cache. Same data source, same SDK, independently reimplemented, no shared cache — worth consolidating eventually, not urgent.
- **Options chain (live):** `options_data.py:get_options_chain()` (via `GetOptionContractsRequest`, 5-35 DTE filter) — confirmed WIRED IN (not dead code, despite looking like an early exploratory script with a hardcoded placeholder price in its `__main__` block): grep for `get_options_chain|filter_near_the_money|pick_best_contract` finds **13 files**, including `live_options_trader.py`, `spy_gamma_execution.py`, `spy_gamma_daytrader.py`, `gamma_regime.py`, `gamma_landscape.py`, `charm_landscape.py`.
- **Live option quotes:** `options_evaluator.py:get_live_premium()` (ask, for entries) / `get_live_bid_price()` (bid, for valuing existing positions) via `OptionHistoricalDataClient`/`OptionLatestQuoteRequest`. Grep confirms **13 call sites** including `live_options_trader.py`, `spy_gamma_execution.py`, `options_exit_monitor.py`, `audit_positions_vs_alpaca.py`.
- **Live contract selection by delta:** `pick_contract_by_delta.py:pick_contract_by_target_delta()` — used ONLY by `live_options_trader.py` (2 files total in grep, the other being its own definition). NOT used by the SPY gamma daytrader path — that system apparently has its own selection logic (unconfirmed, Phase 2+ scope).
- **Account/order data:** `TradingClient.get_account()` / `.get_all_positions()` / `.get_orders()` / `.get_order_by_id()` used directly in `main.py`, `live_trader.py`, `live_options_trader.py`, `daily_price_audit.py` — `daily_price_audit.py` specifically reconciles logged trade prices against Alpaca's real filled-order data daily and auto-corrects the DB (`options_trades.db`) on mismatch, matching entry/exit orders precisely via the order ID embedded in `trade_id` (format `opt-live-{ticker}-{order_id}`).

**EODHD integration:**
- Confirms and extends the existing Section 4 rules (id-date rule, `bid_date` unreliability). `gex_data_loader.py` is the most mature ingestion module found: real JSON:API pagination (follows `links.next`, manually re-appends `api_token` since EODHD's own next-link drops it — already documented in Section 4 — plus an offset-increment fallback and a 20-page circuit breaker), a DB-level `UNIQUE(ticker, snapshot_date, contract)` constraint as a second layer against duplicate insertion, and `get_real_trading_days()` / `get_previous_trading_day()` which discover actual trading days from real Alpaca bar data rather than assuming calendar days. Writes to `gex_data_cache.db` (646MB — the largest DB in the project).
- **Confirmed duplication: two independent, near-identical "safe T-1 historical IV" implementations exist.** `data_layer_iv_helper.py:get_timestamp_safe_historical_iv()` and `v2_iv_pricing_model.py:get_v2_entry_iv()`/`get_v2_checkpoint_iv()` both re-implement the same pattern (query EODHD wide exp_date range → filter `id_date == T-1` → nearest-strike/nearest-DTE selection) independently, with no shared code between them. Grep confirms they're used on completely disjoint call paths: `data_layer_iv_helper.py` is called ONLY by the ML Experiment 1 pipeline (`data_prep_experiment1.py`, `iv_percentile_batch.py`, `features_options.py`, `test_features_options.py`) — which Section 5 already records as **REJECTED** — while `v2_iv_pricing_model.py` feeds the live V1/V2 pricing-correction chain and `portfolio_backtest.py`'s `pricing_mode="v2_corrected"`. Not a bug (the rejected pipeline is dead either way), but if `data_layer_iv_helper.py` is ever revived, it should probably just import `v2_iv_pricing_model.py` instead of maintaining a parallel implementation.
- **`daily_collection_run.py`** feeds `prospective_gex_pipeline.py` (the "call-wall-distance hypothesis" data collection referenced in Section 5) — confirmed via grep to have **zero internal call sites** (nothing in the codebase imports or invokes it). **[UPDATED 2026-09-20: confirmed via `Get-ScheduledTask` — this IS automated, via a real Windows Task Scheduler job, `GEX_Prospective_Daily_Collection`, running `run_gex_collection.bat` daily at 20:00, `WorkingDirectory` = this project root. Not a repo-invisible mystery after all.]** Distinct from and NOT part of `options_shadow_loop.py`'s live loop.

## 9. Architecture map: calculator layer (GEX / gamma / charm / IV / Greeks)

*Phase 2 of the systematic codebase audit (2026-09-20). Phase 1 (Section 8) covered data ingestion. Phases 3-4 (pricing/selection logic feeding off these calculators, everything else) still to come.*

**HEADLINE FINDING: there are two separate, architecturally disconnected gamma/GEX systems in this codebase — a validated research pipeline that no live file uses, and an unvalidated live pipeline that actively drives a real (paper) trading strategy's entry signal.**

**System 1 — EODHD-based, research/backtest-only, no-lookahead-safe (validated methodology, no formal verdict on the signal itself):**
- `gex_calculator.py` (Phase 2 of the GEX pipeline): computes Net GEX (both `model_a` and `flipped` sign conventions), TWO distinct gamma-flip methodologies — Method A (`cumulative_strike_transition`, real/as-observed gamma summed in strike order — research-defined, NOT the standard industry concept) and Method B (`true_zero_gamma`, the standard methodology: sweeps hypothetical spot prices ±20%, at each one RECOMPUTES every contract's Black-Scholes gamma via `options_pricing.py:calculate_gamma()` using real strike/DTE/observed IV, finds the sign crossing) — plus call/put gamma walls and a discrete gamma-gradient-at-spot. Writes to `gex_data_cache.db:gex_metrics`.
- `gex_lookup.py` (Phase 3, now fully read — was docstring-only, open issue #4, now closed): read-only T-1 retrieval for a signal at time T, with an **executable** no-lookahead guarantee (`assert snapshot_date_obj < signal_date` and `== t_minus_1`, not just a design promise). Exposes only Method B as the gamma-flip feature — confirms Section 5's note that Method A was found unstable (docstring here quantifies it: "22/30 ticker-days >50% from spot"). Derives continuous features: `gamma_flip_distance_pct`, `call_wall_distance_pct`, `put_wall_distance_pct`, `net_gex_normalized`.
  - **GEX regime testability — RESOLVED 2026-09-21, per Section 14's source-of-truth rule (code outranks prose).** The live code in `gex_lookup.py` (line 82) hardcodes `is_regime_testable=True` with a comment: "expanded 372-ticker-day dataset confirmed real variation (331 positive / 41 negative), reopened per explicit review." **Searched this whole file for the specific stale wording this note originally flagged ("NOT TESTABLE... 30/30 positive, zero variation") and did not find it as standalone text anywhere else in the current document** — this note itself, plus Section 18's registry row, are now the authoritative record. Testable ≠ validated: no formal experiment has yet evaluated whether GEX regime actually predicts anything (Section 6 item 6 still correctly says no formal verdict exists on any GEX signal).
- **Wired-vs-orphaned, System 1 (grep evidence):** `calculate_gex_for_ticker_day` (grep: `calculate_gex_for_ticker_day|from gex_calculator`) → only `run_phase2_full_scale.py` and `test_gex_calculator.py`. `get_gex_for_signal` (grep: `get_gex_for_signal|from gex_lookup`) → only `prospective_gex_pipeline.py`, `regime_sensitivity_analysis.py`, `phase4_experiment.py`, `phase4_stage1_data_collection.py`, `phase3_revalidation.py`. **Zero live-loop files (`options_shadow_loop.py`, `live_options_trader.py`, `spy_gamma_daytrader.py`, `spy_gamma_shadow_loop.py`) consume this pipeline at all.** It is research/batch-only, exactly as Section 5 already implied — confirmed, not just assumed.
- `gex_health_check.py`, `gex_regime_breakdown.py`: standalone diagnostic scripts (read-only SELECTs; `gex_regime_breakdown.py` has no function/class defs at all — executes immediately top-to-bottom on run, clearly run-directly-only, not designed to be imported).
- `prospective_gex_pipeline.py`: forward-only collector for the call-wall-distance hypothesis, two-phase (`log_signals_for_date` / `backfill_forward_returns`), proper no-lookahead design (excludes-never-substitutes when T-1 GEX unavailable). Resolves open issue #4's "unknown if it has accumulated results" — see Section 7 item 4: **11 rows total**, stale since 2026-09-09.

**System 2 — live-Alpaca-based, hardcoded-volatility, self-caveated estimates, actively wired into live (paper) trading:**
- `gamma_landscape.py`, `gamma_regime.py`, `charm_landscape.py`, `gamma_gradient.py` (builds on `gamma_landscape.py`'s output), `daily_levels.py` (pure price S/R, no options data — the odd one out, only tangentially gamma-related).
- **All four gamma/charm estimators pull live options-chain data from `options_data.py:get_options_chain()` (Alpaca), NOT from EODHD/`gex_data_loader.py`, and all default to a HARDCODED `volatility=0.30` parameter** for their `options_pricing.py:calculate_gamma()`/`calculate_charm()` calls — not real historical IV (`v2_iv_pricing_model.py`) or even a live-quoted IV. Each file's own docstring self-caveats this as "an ESTIMATE, not certain dealer positioning data." This is a real, unflagged inconsistency with the project's validated real-IV methodology — same Greeks math (see below), materially different and unvalidated volatility input.
- **Wired-vs-orphaned, System 2 (grep evidence) — CONFIRMED actively used by live trading, two separate paths:**
  1. `options_confluence_modifier.py:calculate_size_modifier()` (uses `gamma_landscape.py` + `daily_levels.py`) is called by `live_options_trader.py` at its live order-entry point (line 303), intended per its own docstring to scale position size 0.5x–1.5x based on gamma-wall/level proximity. **Verified this is currently a no-op on the live path — a real bug, not just an audit note:** `contracts` is computed at `live_options_trader.py:299`, the `gamma_check` modifier is computed afterward at line 303, and `contracts` is never reassigned using it anywhere before the order is submitted with `qty=contracts` at line 317. The modifier value is only appended to a log string (`entry_reasoning`) and stored as trade-log metadata (`gamma_modifier` field) — it does not actually affect what gets traded, despite the function's docstring and evident intent.
  2. `spy_gamma_daytrader.py:check_spy_gamma_signal()` — a **completely separate live strategy** (SPY-only, paper). Confirmed live wiring chain: `spy_gamma_shadow_loop.py` (the running loop) → `spy_gamma_execution.py:check_and_enter_spy_gamma()` (imports `check_spy_gamma_signal` at line 29) → `spy_gamma_daytrader.py`. Its entry signal is **100% derived from this unvalidated chain**: negative net-gamma regime (`gamma_regime.py`) + price near a gamma wall (`gamma_landscape.py`) confirmed by a steep gradient zone (`gamma_gradient.py`), optionally reinforced by a charm pin (`charm_landscape.py`) or daily S/R level (`daily_levels.py`) confluence. The file's own docstring admits: "HONEST LIMITATION: cannot be backtested (no historical open-interest data). Pure live forward-test from day one." `SPY_GAMMA_SHADOW_MODE = True` is hardcoded at module level (consistent with Phase 1's paper-only finding — no real capital at risk), but this strategy **is** actively trading paper capital right now on a signal chain with no formal verdict, no backtest, and no connection to the project's validation framework (gates/verdict/multiple-testing/robustness) or to System 1's more rigorous EODHD-based pipeline.
- **Naming collision (minor, not a functional bug):** `gex_calculator.py` defines its own internal `find_gamma_walls(contracts, spot_price, sign_convention) -> dict` (System 1, EODHD snapshot data). `gamma_landscape.py` defines an unrelated `find_gamma_walls(landscape, top_n=3) -> list[dict]` (System 2, live Alpaca data). Same name, different modules, different signatures, different data sources — properly namespaced so it doesn't break anything, but a real trap for future find-and-replace or copy-paste mistakes given how similar the two systems' vocabulary is.
- **The Greeks formulas themselves are NOT duplicated** — `options_pricing.py` is the single shared implementation (`black_scholes_price`, `calculate_delta`, `calculate_gamma`, `calculate_vega`, `calculate_charm`, `calculate_theta`), imported directly by `gex_calculator.py`, all four System-2 estimators, `options_backtest_engine.py`, and `live_options_trader.py` itself (for per-trade Greeks logging at entry). The inconsistency is entirely in what volatility value each call site feeds in (real historical IV vs. hardcoded 0.30), not the math. Resolves open issue #5 (Section 7) — see there.
- `entry_scorer.py` — checked, does not import any GEX/gamma/IV calculator (only `backtest_engine` for bars); out of Phase 2 scope, pure signal-scoring logic for Phase 3+.

---

## 10. Architecture map: external integrations (cross-cutting)

*Coordinator-requested addition (2026-09-20), run alongside the phased audit (Sections 8-9) because Phases 1-2 only looked at Alpaca/EODHD inside specific subsystems, never asked "every external service, comprehensively." Confirmed via `.env`: six vars — `ALPACA_API_KEY`/`ALPACA_SECRET_KEY`, `EODHD_API_TOKEN`, `MASSIVE_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `KILL_SERVER_TOKEN`, `TRADING_LOOP`.*

**Alpaca** — fully characterized in Section 8, not re-derived here. Re-grepped to confirm no live-loop file was missed: `import alpaca|from alpaca|alpaca_trade_api|alpaca\.|TradingClient|StockHistoricalDataClient|OptionHistoricalDataClient` still finds exactly the same **21 files** as Section 8, including every live-loop file (`live_options_trader.py`, `spy_gamma_execution.py`, `spy_gamma_daytrader.py`, `daily_price_audit.py`, `options_exit_monitor.py`, `main.py`, `live_trader.py`). **Feeds: both** (live + research), paper-only throughout.

**EODHD** — `EODHD_API_TOKEN|eodhd\.com` finds **41 files**. Of those, only **21** actually import the canonical shared modules (`gex_data_loader.py` / `v2_iv_pricing_model.py`) — a separate grep for the raw-request pattern (`requests.get(BASE_URL...)`) independently finds essentially the *same* 41 files (40/41 overlap), meaning **most of the 41 files construct their own inline HTTP call rather than reusing the shared, id-date-safe modules** — roughly 30 one-off `fix_*`/`diagnose_*`/`verify_*`/`audit_*`/`investigate_*` scripts each duplicate the fetch pattern independently. Consistent with Section 2's note that most of the codebase is one-off research scripts; not a new problem, just now quantified. Cross-checked against every live-loop file (Alpaca's 21 + the calculator layer's live consumers from Section 9) — **zero overlap**. **Feeds: research only, confirmed comprehensively, not just for GEX** (Section 9 already established this for the GEX slice; this confirms it for EODHD as a whole).

**Anthropic/Claude API** — NEW, not previously documented anywhere in this file. **7 files independently instantiate their own `anthropic.Anthropic()` client** — a real, previously-unflagged duplication (`ai_analyst.py`'s shared `_make_client()`/`_ask()` helper exists but is reused by nobody): `ai_analyst.py` (model `claude-opus-4-5`), and `lessons_learned.py`, `spy_gamma_lessons_learned.py`, `account_review.py`, `trade_reviewer.py`, `review_open_position.py`, `pattern_review.py` (all six on `claude-sonnet-5`).
- **Confirmed called FROM the live loop, multiple paths:** `options_shadow_loop.py` (the live orchestrator, Section 8) directly imports `pattern_review.generate_pattern_review` and `account_review.should_run_weekly_account_review`/`generate_account_review`. `options_exit_monitor.py` (live) and `spy_gamma_execution.py` (SPY live) both import `trade_reviewer.review_and_store_trade`, firing on every trade exit.
- **None of it influences a trading decision** — checked every call site's actual purpose: `trade_reviewer.py`'s own docstring states "Does NOT modify any code or strategy"; `review_open_position.py`'s prompt explicitly instructs the model "Do NOT suggest specific code changes"; `pattern_review.py`/`account_review.py`/`spy_gamma_lessons_learned.py` all produce narrative text stored to their own dedicated DBs (`trade_reviews.db`, `pattern_reviews.db`, `account_reviews.db`, `spy_gamma_lessons.db`) for human reading, nothing programmatic downstream.
- **One exception, but confirmed research-only:** `ai_analyst.py:diagnose_and_suggest()` DOES return machine-parsed JSON (`config_changes`) meant to alter parameters for the next iteration — but it's only called from `optimizer.py`, which has **zero Alpaca imports** (cross-checked against the Alpaca 21-file list above), confirming it's a standalone iterative backtest-parameter-search tool, not connected to live trading in any way.
- `review_open_position.py` itself is orphaned (grep for `import review_open_position|from review_open_position` → 0 hits anywhere) — a standalone manual script, not wired into anything, despite its Claude client being live and its helper (`contract_price_extremes.py`) being genuinely live-wired elsewhere (see Massive, below).
- **Feeds: both** (live loop calls it repeatedly, research/optimizer calls it too) — but strictly for narrative/logging on the live side, and for a self-contained backtest-tuning loop on the research side. No live capital-affecting decision is made by Claude anywhere audited.

**"Massive" data source** — NEW, never appeared in this file before. `MASSIVE_API_KEY` usage (`from massive import RESTClient`) confirmed in exactly **6 files**: `test_massive_data.py`, `contract_price_extremes.py`, `check_contract_peak.py`, `check_today_peak.py`, `check_morning_peak.py`, `update_check_contract_peak.py` — all last modified **2026-08-26, the same single day**, looking at first glance like an abandoned one-day experiment. It is a Polygon.io-compatible REST client (`client.list_aggs(ticker="O:...", timespan="minute", ...)`, OCC options symbols) providing **real intraday (minute-bar) OPTION price history** — data neither Alpaca (underlying-focused) nor EODHD (EOD-only options, already documented) provides.
- **Surprising finding — this is NOT abandoned, it IS live-wired, the same shape of gap as the shadow-trading system itself:** `contract_price_extremes.py:get_contract_price_extremes()` is called directly from `options_exit_monitor.py` (the live exit-monitoring loop) at every position exit (lines 160-164 there) to compute the real intraday peak/trough the contract hit, feeding that into `review_trade_data["price_extremes"]` — which then goes into `trade_reviewer.py`'s Claude-based post-hoc review (above). So Massive data reaches the live loop on every single trade exit. **Scope check, important:** it is enrichment for the post-hoc narrative only — the exit decision itself has already been made by the time this runs; Massive does not influence entries, exits, or sizing anywhere audited.
- **Red herring, worth recording so it isn't rechecked later:** `implement_hybrid_volatility.py` matched an early case-insensitive `massive` grep, but only because a comment says volatility noise "gets **massively** amplified" — it does not import or use the Massive vendor at all. Its "hybrid volatility" method (already live in `options_backtest_engine.py:calculate_historical_volatility()`) is pure EWMA smoothing over existing Alpaca daily-bar closes, explicitly self-caveated in its own docstring as "a real, principled ESTIMATE... NOT real market-implied volatility."
- **Feeds: live** (post-hoc trade-review enrichment only, not decisions) **+ a dormant one-day research exploration** (the 5 "peak" scripts besides `contract_price_extremes.py` itself appear to be that exploration's leftover tooling, not otherwise wired anywhere — not individually re-verified beyond the import-site grep above, low priority to chase further).

**Misc env vars:** `KILL_SERVER_TOKEN` — auth token gating `kill_server.py`/`options_kill_server.py`'s kill switch, already known, not an external data/decision service. `TRADING_LOOP` — `main.py:40`: `LOOP = os.getenv("TRADING_LOOP", "0") == "1"`; a comment there says it's set to `"1"` in the deployed VM's secrets. **Open question, not resolved in this pass:** this implies `main.py` has its own independently deployment-gated loop, separate from `options_shadow_loop.py` (which Section 8 identified as *the* live orchestrator) — worth confirming in a later phase whether both run in production or only one is actually deployed.

## 11. Architecture map: pricing, selection, and execution logic

*Phase 3 of the systematic codebase audit (2026-09-20). Phase 1 (Section 8) covered data ingestion, Phase 2 (Section 9) covered the calculator layer, and the coordinator-requested external-integrations pass (Section 10) ran alongside. Phase 4 (everything else, lower priority) still to come. Run as two parallel sub-passes to keep pace given file count; merged here by the coordinator.*

**HEADLINE FINDING 1 — the options backtest that produced V1's frozen +$20,427.64 result (Section 3) never simulated the account-level risk controls the live system actually enforces.** `risk_manager.py`'s `check_max_positions()` (cap 3), `check_daily_loss_limit()` (3%), and `check_drawdown_pause()` (8%) are imported and enforced in all three live paths (`live_trader.py` stock, `live_options_trader.py` options, `spy_gamma_execution.py` SPY) and in the stock backtest (`backtest_engine.py`) — but grep finds **zero matches** for `risk_manager`, `check_max_positions`, `check_daily_loss_limit`, `check_drawdown_pause`, or `calculate_position_size` anywhere in `options_backtest_engine.py` or `portfolio_backtest.py`. V1's 251-trade sequence was never checked against position-count, daily-loss, or drawdown limits — a real, previously undocumented realism gap distinct from the pricing/execution corrections Section 3 already tracks. Candidate follow-up: quantify how many of V1's 251 trades would have been blocked had these three checks been simulated.

**HEADLINE FINDING 2 — the live options path has no trailing-stop and no dynamic ATR-based stop/target, despite both existing in the codebase.** `options_trailing_stop.py` (gain-scaled trail) and `dynamic_options_thresholds.py` (ATR-derived stop/target via option delta) are both backtest-only — grep finds only `options_backtest_engine.py` and themselves, zero live files. Confirmed by direct read of `live_options_trader.py`: its live bracket order uses a hardcoded static config (`"stop_loss_pct": 0.40, "take_profit_pct": 0.75`, lines 65-66/401-402). The live options path's only "protect the gain" mechanism is `reversal_detector.py`'s profit-protection check (active only once unrealized gain ≥40%, exits on a 5-bar MA cross-back).

**HEADLINE FINDING 3 — `options_shadow_loop.py`'s "no real orders" safety claim is stale and currently false (see the correction added to Section 8 above).** `live_options_trader.py:60` sets `OPTIONS_SHADOW_MODE = False  # LIVE as of first supervised test`; when the market is open it calls `_trading_client.submit_order()` for real (paper) market orders and logs "LIVE ORDER SUBMITTED". `options_exit_monitor.py`'s close logic does the same on the exit side. Paper-only throughout (Section 8's `paper=True`-everywhere finding still holds, so no capital is at risk), but the orchestrator's own name, docstring, and console output ("paper analysis only, NO orders placed") no longer describe what the code does. By contrast, `spy_gamma_daytrader.py`'s `SPY_GAMMA_SHADOW_MODE = True` was verified as genuinely current — `spy_gamma_execution.py` returns early before `submit_order()` in both its entry and exit paths. That system's shadow-mode claim is accurate.

**HEADLINE FINDING 4 — production deployment topology, confirmed via `.replit`, not just code inference.** Only the stock bot (`main.py` → `live_trader.py`) appears in `.replit`'s `[deployment]` auto-restart config (`run = ["python", "main.py"]`, `TRADING_LOOP = "1"` under `[userenv.production]`). `options_shadow_loop.py` and `spy_gamma_shadow_loop.py` are confirmed running (Section 8's DB-mtime evidence, plus their hardcoded kill-server ports 8081/8099 matching ports `.replit` explicitly opens) but are **not** part of the declared deployment config — meaning they run as manually-started or otherwise-unaccounted-for companion processes. **How they actually get started is not visible anywhere in the repo — an open question for the user, not resolvable by further code reading.**

*(Resolves the Section 10 `TRADING_LOOP` open question: it only toggles `main.py`'s own loop-vs-single-pass mode for the stock bot — no third live system exists. `main.py` never imports `live_options_trader` or any options logic.)*

### Entry, selection, and risk/exit logic

**Entry scoring — live-wired.** `entry_scorer.py:calculate_entry_score()` is a continuous 0-100 weighted score (relative volume 0-25, breakout magnitude 0-20, trend strength 0-20, momentum 0-15, volatility regime 0-10, news sentiment 0-5, SPY regime alignment 0-5), replacing an older binary gate. Its own docstring explicitly excludes gamma/charm from the score "since they cannot be backtested... and including them would make the score itself partially unvalidatable" — consistent with Section 9's System 2 caveats. Grep confirms **31 files**, including `live_options_trader.py` — genuinely live-wired.

**Delta-based strike selection — backtest-only, confirms Section 5's existing tag.** `delta_strike_finder.py:find_strike_for_target_delta()` — grep finds 9 files, all backtest/research, zero live-loop files. Live selection goes entirely through `pick_contract_by_delta.py` (Section 8) instead; the two never overlap.

**Reversal detection — live-wired, exit-side.** `reversal_detector.py:check_reversal_signal()` (5-bar MA cross-back, active only once unrealized gain ≥40%) is imported directly by `options_exit_monitor.py` (live). Its patch history (`refine_reversal_detector.py`, `final_fix_reversal_detector.py`, `apply_reversal_guard_fix.py`) is historical one-off scripts, not live-imported.

**Ticker circuit breaker — live-wired, both options live paths.** `ticker_circuit_breaker.py:check_ticker_circuit_breaker()` (blocks new signals after 3 consecutive real losses on a ticker, manual-clear only, reads `options_trades.db` directly) is used by both `live_options_trader.py` and `spy_gamma_execution.py`.

**Time-decay exit — backtest-only, so no live exposure, but flags an unresolved documentation question.** `time_decay_exit.py:check_time_decay_exit()` combines theta-burn-rate with a live re-scored win-probability from `baseline_model.joblib`. Grep finds only `options_backtest_engine.py` and historical patch scripts — zero live files. Not resolved in this pass: whether `baseline_model.joblib` is literally ML Experiment 1's rejected model artifact (Section 5) or a separate model.

**Position sizing is not shared between stock and options paths.** `risk_manager.calculate_position_size()` (equity-risk-based share count) is used only by `live_trader.py` and `backtest_engine.py` (both stock-only). `live_options_trader.py` computes contract count with its own inline formula (`contracts = max(int(max_risk_dollars / (premium * 100)), 1)`, line 299) — not a bug (options need whole-contract counts, not shares), but means the options live path's sizing logic has no backtest-side equivalent verified against it. The already-documented position-size-modifier bug (Section 9 — `options_confluence_modifier.py`'s gamma modifier computed but never applied) sits downstream of this.

**`exit_tracker.py` is a separate, stock-only system — an architectural distinction to remember, not a bug.** It pairs with `main.py`/`live_trader.py` (parses trade IDs as `"live-{ticker}-{order_id}"`), entirely distinct from the options exit path (`options_exit_monitor.py`, which uses `"opt-live-{ticker}-{order_id}"` IDs and its own imports: `options_trade_log`, `trade_reviewer`, `contract_price_extremes`, `options_evaluator`, `reversal_detector`, `overnight_hold_check`). If `exit_tracker.py` were ever pointed at `options_trades.db` its ID parsing would silently skip every row — but it never is.

### Backtest engine and live orchestration

**`live_trader.py` (stock) vs. `live_options_trader.py` (options) — parallel strategies on different instruments, neither supersedes the other.** Same signal family (breakout/volume/regime, `risk_manager.py` gates) reused on both. `live_trader.py` uses native Alpaca bracket orders (`OrderClass.BRACKET`, Alpaca manages the exit). `live_options_trader.py` can't — Alpaca doesn't support bracket orders for options — so `options_exit_monitor.py` exists specifically to actively poll and manage exits itself (confirmed via that file's own docstring).

**`options_exit_monitor.py`'s exit-decision logic** (beyond its already-documented Massive/Claude calls in Section 10): five exit reasons checked in order — `stop_loss`, `reversal_guard` (≥40% gain + `reversal_detector` confirmation), `take_profit`, `expiry_safety_net` (force-close ≤2 days before expiry to avoid auto-exercise/assignment), `overnight_risk` (last 30 min of trading day). Real fill-price polling (10×1s against `get_order_by_id()`) on both entry and exit, falling back to the pre-trade estimate only if the order never confirms in time.

**`spy_gamma_execution.py`'s own execution/sizing logic** (beyond Section 9's signal-generation coverage): flat position sizing (`equity × POSITION_RISK_PCT / (premium × 100)`), contract selection via `options_data.py`'s basic chain filter + highest-open-interest pick — **not** `pick_contract_by_delta.py` — closing Section 8's "unconfirmed" note that this system has its own selection logic. Distinctive: 4-way scale-out tranching (`_build_tranches()`: A/B/C at 20%/40%/75% take-profit, plus an uncapped RUNNER tranche exited only via a stricter ≥50%-gain reversal guard), each tranche logged/exited independently. Force-closes all SPY positions at 15:45 ET regardless of P&L (day-trade-only, matches the strategy's documented no-overnight-hold design).

**`portfolio_backtest.py` does not call `options_backtest_engine.py`'s main function** — it reimplements its own chronological multi-ticker event loop (`PortfolioAccount` class: shared cash, `can_afford()` capital gating, mark-to-market equity curve) but directly reuses three specific helpers from `options_backtest_engine.py`: `calculate_historical_volatility()` (the "hybrid" EWMA-smoothed vol — this closes Section 10's `implement_hybrid_volatility.py` red-herring note: this hybrid methodology **is** the one actually live in the codebase, under this different filename, not a separate abandoned exploration), `black_scholes_price()`, and `_estimated_spread_cost_pct()` (a simpler, self-caveated spread estimate, distinct from the real bid/ask work in the V1/V2 correction chain). `portfolio_backtest.py`'s inline comments document at least four previously-fixed discrepancy-hunting bugs versus the original engine (a missing SPY-regime cache, a stray entry-index gate, a doubled-entry-opportunity bug, asymmetric entry/exit spread friction) — all already fixed in current code, not open issues.

---

## 12. Architecture map: remaining infrastructure, research framework, and audit closure (Phase 4)

*Phase 4 of the systematic codebase audit (2026-09-20) — the final phase ("everything else, lower priority" per the original plan). Run as two parallel sub-passes, merged here by the coordinator. This closes out the phased audit: Sections 8-12 now cover data ingestion, the calculator layer, external integrations, pricing/selection/execution logic, and this pass's remaining live-support infrastructure, the formal research framework, and a full live-import closure check.*

### Live-support infrastructure and import-closure check

**Closure check result: the live-reachable import graph is now fully traced, and it surfaced two genuinely new, previously-undocumented live-wired modules — neither is a live-decision risk.** Method: recursively grepped local imports starting from all 8 live-loop entry points (`main.py`, `live_trader.py`, `live_options_trader.py`, `options_shadow_loop.py`, `options_exit_monitor.py`, `spy_gamma_daytrader.py`, `spy_gamma_shadow_loop.py`, `spy_gamma_execution.py`) until no new local module appeared. Every module reached was already named in Sections 8-11 **except**:

- **A third, previously undocumented ML pipeline reaches live code — confirmed non-decision-affecting.** `feature_extraction.py` (13 features: delta/gamma/theta/charm/vega_per_1pct_iv, realized_volatility(+percentile), relative_volume, breakout/trend/momentum scores, moneyness, dte) plus `baseline_model.joblib` (a logistic regression trained by `save_trained_model.py`, itself a standalone script with zero internal call sites, run manually) is a **separate pipeline from ML Experiment 1's Feature Factory** (Section 5) — different file, different feature set, confirmed via `train_baseline_model.py` having zero references to either `feature_extraction` or `baseline_model.joblib`. This model is imported live in two places, both confirmed by direct code read to be pure shadow-logging: `live_options_trader.py` runs `predict_proba` and logs to `ml_shadow_predictions.db` *after* the real order is already submitted, explicitly commented "purely additive logging, NEVER affects the actual trading decision," wrapped in a bare `except: pass` "so shadow logging failure must NEVER block the real trade"; `spy_gamma_execution.py` calls `extract_features_live()` only to enrich a trade-log JSON field, no model even loaded there. The only place this model's prediction actually *gates* a decision is `time_decay_exit.py` (Section 11, confirmed backtest-only).
- **`forward_test_logger.py` is called hourly by the deployed stock bot** (`main.py:235`, inside `run_live_trading_loop()` — confirmed as the actual deployed loop per Section 11) — it computes `options_confluence_modifier.calculate_size_modifier()` (the same gamma modifier Section 9 found broken/no-op on the *options* live path) and `gamma_regime.calculate_net_gamma_regime()` for each stock ticker, logging both to `gamma_forward_test.db` (explains that DB's freshness from Section 1). Pure logging — the stock bot's own entry logic never uses gamma (`entry_scorer.py` explicitly excludes it). Net effect: the broken gamma-modifier signal is quietly being forward-tested via passive snapshots on the stock side, independent of any real trade outcome.

No other gaps found — everything else in the closure traces cleanly to what Sections 8-11 already document.

**`options_kill_server.py` — a positive contrast to the `options_shadow_loop.py` finding.** Its status page reads `OPTIONS_SHADOW_MODE` at render time and already correctly displays "LIVE -- real orders active" when the flag is `False` — never went stale, unlike `options_shadow_loop.py`'s docstring/console output (corrected above, Section 8). Confirmed `kill_server.py` (stock, port 8080) and `options_kill_server.py` (options, port 8081) share the exact same `STOP_TRADING` file — stopping either pauses both systems.

**`overnight_hold_check.py`** — two checks: direction-aware news sentiment, and an ATR volatility-spike check (unsafe if current ATR > 1.75× trailing-20-bar average). **Self-documented limitation: it cannot check an earnings calendar** — earnings gaps are, by its own docstring, "the single biggest real overnight gap-risk factor for options," and this check has no data source for it. Partial safeguard only, by design admission.

**`account_review.py`** (full logic; Section 10 covered only its Claude usage) — pulls 7-day/30-day closed-trade windows, computes calls-vs-puts and same-day-vs-multi-day win-rates, exit-reason distribution, and a gamma-modifier win-rate correlation, then asks Claude to analyze trend/calibration (instructed not to suggest code changes). Self-documented limitation: the gamma-modifier correlation isn't cleanly joined to trade records yet.

**`uptime_log.py`/`automated_uptime_monitor.py`** — confirmed live-wired, working as documented; the monitor cross-references downtime gaps against `options_trades.db` to flag only gaps that overlapped an actually-open position, filtering out routine market-closed gaps.

**`trade_log.py` (stock) vs. `options_trade_log.py` (options)** — confirmed genuinely parallel, distinct schemas (consistent with the `exit_tracker.py`/`options_exit_monitor.py` split in Section 11). Stock schema carries a process-review layer (`outcome_class`, `root_cause`, `was_process_correct`) the options schema lacks; options schema instead carries `gamma_modifier`/`gamma_context`. Neither reads the other's database.

### Research framework, ML pipeline status, and audit coverage reconciliation

**The formal research framework (`experiment_config.py`, `gates.py`, `verdict.py`, `multiple_testing.py`, `robustness.py`, `run_experiment.py`, `canonical_hash.py`, `db_interface.py`, `experiment.py`, `create_research_db_schema.py`) is real, well-built, and genuinely enforces Section 1's philosophy** — immutable canonically-hashed configs, a locked `applicability_lock` gates.py reads from exclusively, a three-state gate model with `InconsistentGateStateError` guarding invalid states, append-only gate history, real Bonferroni correction (Benjamini-Hochberg explicitly raises `BenjaminiHochbergNotSupportedError` rather than partially implementing it), a three-state robustness rollup where undeclared checks count as insufficient (not vacuously passed), and `run_experiment.py` has zero imports of any V1/live-trading file — structurally incapable of touching V1.

**Queried `research_database.db` directly — usage confirmed precisely: 8 total rows in `experiments`.** Only **one** ever ran through the real `preregister_experiment()` → `run_experiment()` pipeline with a real verdict: `ml_experiment1_feature_factory_vs_baseline_20260917` (two verdict rows, both `REJECTED`) — confirms the framework-to-ML-Experiment-1 connection Section 5 implied but never verified directly. **Three rows** (`phase6_signal_random_control_20260914`, `phase7_pnl_concentration_20260914`, `full_population_iv_repricing_20260914`) were backfilled directly, never actually processed through gates/verdict — don't mistake these for framework-validated verdicts. **Four rows** are test-suite fixture artifacts (`test_run_experiment.py` writes directly to the production `research_database.db`, no test isolation) — a minor hygiene issue, harmless but worth knowing before running `SELECT * FROM experiments` expecting only real research. **Answers Section 6 issues #6/#7: yes, this framework is ready-to-use, currently-idle infrastructure** — one successful real use, works as designed, available for whichever EODHD hypothesis or ML method gets picked next.

**`baseline_model.joblib` (open question from Section 11/Phase 3a), resolved: NOT ML Experiment 1's rejected model — a separate, simpler artifact.** `train_baseline_model.py`/`save_trained_model.py` train a 13-feature logistic regression via `feature_extraction.py` (see closure-check finding above) — architecturally distinct from ML Experiment 1's 11-feature Feature Factory, confirmed via import-graph (never imported by or importing each other).

**ML Experiment 1 pipeline (light-touch grep confirmation, not re-read — Section 5 already documents REJECTED/robustness-tested):** every import of its ~12 files terminates in another ML-Experiment-1 file or a research/test script. **Zero live-loop files import any of them.** Confirmed dormant, matching Section 5.

**Final coverage reconciliation.** Recounted: **317 `.py` files**, matches Section 2 exactly (not stale). Across all 4 phases, roughly **75-85 files were individually read in full (~24-27% of the codebase)** — data ingestion, calculators, external integrations, entry/selection/risk/exit logic, backtest engine, live orchestration, live-support infrastructure, and the research framework. A further ~15-20 files (ML Experiment 1) were confirmed via targeted grep rather than full reads. **The remaining ~200 files (roughly two-thirds) are the one-off `fix_*`/`patch_*`/`check_*`/`diagnose_*`/`verify_*`/`audit_*`/`investigate_*` research-history bucket Section 2 already describes** — a comprehensive grep (`^(from|import) (fix|patch|check|diagnose|verify|audit|investigate)_[a-zA-Z0-9_]*\b`, all 317 files) returns **zero matches**, structurally confirming none of these scripts is ever imported by anything else — real evidence they're inert, not an assumption carried over. Their *individual* correctness (whether each one's one-time finding was right) remains taken on Section 5's existing word, not re-verified file-by-file.

---

## 13. Root-cause synthesis: does V1 have genuine, repeatable edge? (2026-09-21)

**Verdict: BOTH genuine signal failure AND destruction by pricing-execution economics — independently, additively, neither one fully explains it alone.** Plus a data-limited caveat for one specific subset. Full evidence below.

**New this pass — same-day vs. multi-day split of the combined correction (`combined_iv_spread_correction_v2.py`'s -$45,477.00/248 trades, full rerun 2026-09-21, exact reproduction confirmed against the previously-cited number):**

| Group | n | Total P&L | Win rate | Avg P&L/trade |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Same-day (entry_date == exit_date) | 71 | **-$19,010.00** | **0.0%** | -$267.75 |
| Multi-day | 177 | **-$26,467.00** | 33.3% | -$149.53 |
| Combined | 248 | -$45,477.00 | — | — |

**Important methodological read on the same-day bucket: 0.0% win rate is close to mechanically guaranteed, not purely a signal finding.** As established earlier this session, a same-day trade's entry-ask and exit-bid come from the *literal same EOD snapshot* — so its P&L is `(bid − ask) × contracts × 100`, which is ≤0 by definition whenever bid ≤ ask (true for essentially every liquid quote). This is a **data-limited** artifact of EOD-only granularity applied to a zero-holding-period trade, not primarily evidence the entry signal failed on those specific 71 occasions. It does, however, mean same-day trades contribute a disproportionate, near-certain loss (41.8% of the total loss from 28.6% of trades) purely from spread-crossing mechanics.

**Even removing that entire bucket, the "real" multi-day trades still lose money on average** — 33.3% win rate, -$149.53/trade, -$26,467.00 total over 177 trades. This is the economically meaningful number: it isn't a measurement artifact, it reflects real price movement between entry and exit repriced with real historical IV and real spread costs.

**The four-way synthesis:**

1. **Phase 6 (Section 5)** tests the raw entry signal's predictive power directly and in isolation — no pricing or execution involved at all, just: does a qualifying signal predict real forward returns better than a matched random control? Result: **no** (T_obs=-0.2569%, p=0.996-0.997, reproduced exactly by rerunning the script fresh this session). This is a pure signal-quality finding.
2. **Phase 7 concentration (Section 5)** shows V1's *original, synthetic* +$20,427.64 was never broad-based — **97.7% of it came from 5 trades out of 251.** A signal with genuine, repeatable edge would show positive expectancy distributed across many trades, not near-total dependence on a handful of outliers. This is independent corroboration of finding 1, from the P&L side rather than the statistical-test side: the backtest's own profit structure looks exactly like what a no-edge signal occasionally producing lucky extreme moves would look like.
3. **The V2 IV-only correction (Section 3)** — correcting *only* the volatility input (real historical IV instead of V1's realized-vol proxy), touching *nothing* about execution/spread — is enough by itself to flip the result from +$20,427.64 to **-$10,072.44/237 trades.** This shows V1's synthetic pricing methodology was independently flattering the backtest, on top of whatever the signal itself was or wasn't doing.
4. **The same-day/multi-day split (this pass)** shows that even after isolating out the ~42%-of-the-loss same-day artifact (data-limited, not a signal or execution-economics finding), the remaining, cleanly-interpretable multi-day trades **still lose money on average** once priced with real IV and real spread. Pricing-execution economics alone, independent of the signal question, is sufficient to destroy V1's apparent profitability.

**Why "both," not one or the other:** Findings 1-2 establish the entry signal itself has no demonstrated edge and the original backtest's apparent profit was a fragile, concentrated artifact — that alone would already prevent calling V1 "working." Findings 3-4 establish independently that even setting the signal-quality question aside, realistic pricing and execution costs are, on their own, sufficient to turn the result negative. Neither finding depends on the other being true; they compound. Per this project's defined failure taxonomy (Section 1): **primary categories are genuine signal failure and destroyed by pricing-execution economics, jointly** — with a secondary **data-limited** note on the same-day subset specifically (EOD-only granularity structurally cannot produce a fair same-day P&L estimate, independent of whether the signal is good or bad).

**What this does NOT yet establish:** whether some *other* signal construction (different entry logic, different holding-period discipline, excluding same-day trades by design rather than as an artifact) could show real edge — that's a different, not-yet-asked question. This synthesis is specifically about whether *V1 as currently defined* has genuine edge, and the answer is no, for two independent reasons.

---

## 16. Single-ticker runs vs. true portfolio — critical distinction

**Do not call the +$20,427.64 result a true portfolio return.** Here is exactly what it is instead, verified directly from source (Section 15's citations) plus the actual invocation pattern used to reproduce it this session.

- **`PortfolioAccount` genuinely supports shared capital** — one `cash` pool, `can_afford()` gates every new position against it, a real equity curve via `mark_to_market()`. This is real, working infrastructure, not vaporware.
- **But the frozen $20,427.64/251-trade number was produced by calling `run_portfolio_backtest([ticker], ...)` once per ticker, with a single-element ticker list each time**, then summing the six independent results afterward (`combined_iv_spread_correction_v2.py:60-64`, and this session's reproductions of it). Each ticker therefore got its **own, fully independent $100,000 starting capital**, never competing with any other ticker for cash or a position slot.
- **Consequence: cash constraints are never meaningfully tested.** `can_afford()` is still called and could theoretically block an entry, but only if a single ticker's own trades ever exceeded its own $100k pool — which the 1%-of-capital position sizing formula makes extremely unlikely on its own (worth checking directly if ever in doubt; not separately re-verified here).
- **Simultaneous positions across tickers are structurally unconstrained by capital** (each ticker has its own pool) and **simultaneous positions within a ticker are hard-blocked at 1** (engine rule, not an invocation artifact — see Section 15's last row).
- **Whether the reported P&L is economically comparable to one real portfolio: no.** A real trader running this strategy with $100k total (not $600k) would face real cash competition across 6 tickers that this number never modeled. The reported $20,427.64 implicitly assumes $600,000 of total notional capital available across the period (6 × $100k), not $100k.
- **Position sizing resets independently per ticker** — each ticker's 1%-of-capital sizing is 1% of *its own* $100k, not 1% of a shared pool that shrinks/grows as other tickers win or lose.

**Quantification — read-only simulation against the real 251-trade sequence, run as one hypothetical shared $100k account (script + full trade-level cache saved to scratchpad; methodology and an important caveat below):**
- **Total capital notionally deployed (sum of entry cost across all 251 trades, cumulative over the period, capital recycled as positions close): $220,522.78.** Per-ticker: AAPL $25,407.96, TSLA $31,999.37, NVDA $32,576.65, AMD $41,892.26, SMCI $44,449.55, MSTR $44,196.99 — every ticker stayed comfortably under its own $100k pool cumulatively, confirming `can_afford()` was essentially never a binding constraint for any single ticker in isolation.
- **If run as one single shared $100k account** (summing all 6 tickers' realized P&L into one sequential ledger, ordered by exit date): final equity $120,427.64, matching the same +$20,427.64 total (correct — sum-invariant regardless of account structure). **Zero daily-loss-limit (3%) triggers and zero drawdown-pause (8%) triggers**, using realized P&L applied at each trade's exit date. This specific result is trustworthy as computed — it doesn't depend on the same-day granularity issue below. Caveat: this uses realized P&L snapshotted at exit, not a true continuously-updating mark-to-market equity curve including unrealized P&L on positions still open — a more precise simulation would track intraday unrealized swings too, which could reveal daily-loss or drawdown breaches this simplified version can't see.
- **Max simultaneous open positions (date-level granularity): 13, with 88 of 90 total trading days showing more than 3 concurrently open.** **Important methodological caveat, flagged rather than presented as exact:** the engine's own rule caps each ticker at 1 open position at a time (Section 15), so the true theoretical maximum simultaneous count across 6 tickers is 6, not 13. The measured value of 13 exceeding that theoretical ceiling confirms this quick analysis has some same-day-handoff double-counting (entry_date/exit_date are calendar dates, not bar timestamps, so a same-day exit-then-reentry on one ticker gets counted as "open" on both of that ticker's trades that day). **The qualitative conclusion — V1 would have breached a max-3-positions cap on the large majority of trading days if run as one account — is almost certainly directionally correct** (88/90 days is not a number small overcounting artifacts would manufacture from nothing), but the precise trade-blocked-count this same script computed (251/251, i.e. literally every trade) should be treated as an upper-bound artifact of the same granularity issue, not a validated exact figure. A true bar-level re-simulation would be needed for an exact count — not done here, flagged as a candidate follow-up in Section 28.

**This is one of the highest-priority methodological limitations in the project** — every V1/V2 dollar figure in Section 3 inherits this same limitation, since none of the pricing corrections change the underlying capital model.

## 20. Data lineage

**Underlying prices:** Alpaca (5-min IEX bars) → `backtest_engine.get_historical_bars()` (canonical, cached, 78 call sites) → signal generation (`entry_scorer.py`, breakout/breakdown) → backtest/live execution. **Affects trading decisions: yes, directly, everywhere.**

**Historical option data:** EODHD → raw JSON:API records (id-date-safe filtering, Section 4) → `v2_iv_pricing_model.py`'s `_select_real_contract()` → contract records with bid/ask/IV/OI/Greeks → feeds (a) the V2 pricing-correction chain (Section 17), (b) `gex_calculator.py`/`gex_lookup.py`'s System 1 GEX pipeline → `gex_data_cache.db`. **Affects trading decisions: no — confirmed comprehensively (Section 10), zero live-loop consumption of EODHD for anything.**

**Intraday option data:** Massive → `contract_price_extremes.py` → `options_exit_monitor.py`'s post-hoc review payload → `trade_reviewer.py` (Claude narrative) → `trade_reviews.db`. **Affects trading decisions: no — enrichment for the already-completed exit decision only, confirmed by direct read (Section 10).**

**GEX (historical/research):** EODHD → `gex_data_loader.py` (pagination + trading-calendar helpers) → `gex_calculator.py` (Net GEX, gamma-flip via two methodologies, walls, gradient) → `gex_data_cache.db:gex_metrics` → `gex_lookup.py` (T-1 read-only retrieval) → research/batch consumers only (`prospective_gex_pipeline.py`, `regime_sensitivity_analysis.py`, phase3/4 scripts). **Affects trading decisions: no.**

**GEX (live, System 2):** Alpaca options chains (live) → `gamma_landscape.py`/`gamma_regime.py`/`charm_landscape.py`/`gamma_gradient.py` (hardcoded `volatility=0.30`, not real IV) → `spy_gamma_daytrader.py`'s entry signal → `spy_gamma_execution.py` → real (paper) order submission. **Affects trading decisions: yes — this is the one place an unvalidated signal directly drives live paper capital (Tier-1 item, Section 22).**

**Prospective GEX:** `daily_collection_run.py` (now timezone-fixed) → `prospective_gex_pipeline.py:log_signals_for_date()` → T-1 GEX attached via the same `gex_lookup.py` System 1 pipeline → `prospective_gex_validation.db` → `backfill_forward_returns()` fills in real future returns once elapsed. **Affects trading decisions: no — pure forward-only data collection, feeds no live or backtest decision.**

**AI/Claude:** Trade/account/pattern data (from `trades.db`/`options_trades.db`/signal logs) → one of 7 independently-instantiated `anthropic.Anthropic()` clients → narrative text → `trade_reviews.db`/`pattern_reviews.db`/`account_reviews.db`/`lessons_learned.db`. One exception, `ai_analyst.py:diagnose_and_suggest()`, returns machine-parsed JSON — but only consumed by `optimizer.py`, confirmed zero Alpaca imports (research-only). **Affects trading decisions: no, anywhere, confirmed by reading every call site's actual purpose (Section 10).**

## 21. Lookahead-bias audit

For each major component, exactly what timestamp/date is allowed and what's prohibited:

| Component | Allowed | Prohibited | Enforcement |
|---|---|---|---|
| Signal generation (breakout/score) | Bars up to and including the current checkpoint | Any future bar | `check_lookahead_bias.py` — directly audited, confirmed clean (Section 5) |
| Regime (SPY/ticker daily) | Daily closes up to the current day, 50-day trailing MA | Future closes | Computed from the same historical-bars pull, no separate future-data path found |
| Volatility (V1) | Realized vol from bars up to the current checkpoint (20-day lookback, EWMA-smoothed) | Future bars | Confirmed by direct read, `options_backtest_engine.py:50-139` |
| Option selection/pricing (V1) | Synthetic — no real dated option record involved at all, so no lookahead risk of this specific kind (a different risk: whether the underlying vol/price inputs are contemporaneous, covered above) | N/A | N/A |
| Option selection/pricing (V2, EODHD-based) | The **id-date rule** (Section 4): record's `id` field date suffix must exactly equal the observation date; `tradetime` and `bid_date` are both confirmed unreliable and must never be used for day identification | A record whose `id`-date doesn't match, even if `tradetime` does | `_select_real_contract()`/`get_v2_checkpoint_iv()` hard-filter on this; violated once (17/105 trades) in the now-invalidated naive script (Section 19) |
| GEX (historical) | T-1 only — `gex_lookup.py`'s `get_gex_for_signal()` | Same-day or future GEX | **Executable** assert: `assert snapshot_date_obj < signal_date` and `== t_minus_1`, not just a design promise (Section 9) |
| GEX (prospective) | T-1 only, via the same `gex_lookup.py` path | Same-day or future GEX; missing GEX is excluded, never substituted | `prospective_gex_pipeline.py`'s own design (Section 9); confirmed still true after the 2026-09-20 timezone fix |
| Exits | Bars up to and including the current checkpoint only | Future bars | Same engine loop as entry, no separate exit-side lookahead path found |
| Forward returns (Phase 6/7, prospective backfill) | **Explicitly exempted by design** — constructing a forward-return label or a Monte Carlo null/control distribution requires real future data, used deliberately and only after it has actually elapsed | Using a forward return to influence anything about signal generation or entry/exit logic itself (never observed) | This is the historical-retrospective-research exemption below |

**Two distinct regimes, not to be confused (per the user's explicit instruction this session):**
- **Historical/retrospective research** may use future dates deliberately and explicitly, when pre-specified — e.g. Phase 6's forward 20-bar return, Phase 7's exit-outcome attribution, `prospective_gex_pipeline.py`'s `backfill_forward_returns()`. This is not lookahead bias; it's the actual point of measuring an outcome.
- **Prospective/live validation must never use future information at the time a decision is logged.** Confirmed nowhere in the audited live-loop or prospective-collection code does a "future" value leak into a signal or entry/exit decision — every violation found and fixed this session (the id-date bug, the timezone bug) was a *data-freshness/identification* bug, not a case of genuine future information being deliberately used.

---

## 22. Statistical methodology map

Documented conventions, with honest gaps flagged where not confirmed this session:

- **Unit of observation, Phase 6:** a qualifying signal occurrence (not a trade, not a ticker-day) — 579 valid signals across 60 days, 6 tickers.
- **Calendar-day clustering:** the primary unit in Phase 6's test. **Why:** multiple tickers/signals on the same calendar day share the same SPY regime and often correlated market moves — treating each signal as fully independent would understate the true variance and overstate significance. Clustering by day is the conservative, correct choice given this correlation structure. (This rationale is inferred from the standard statistical reason for such clustering and the project's own emphasis on avoiding overstated significance — **not a direct quote from a script docstring**, flagged per Section 14's rule against presenting interpretation as fact.)
- **Monte Carlo controls:** 1000 replicates, 2 seeds (42, 43) for reproducibility checking. Ticker+regime-conditioned control (matches real signals' ticker and regime) and a secondary same-ticker-same-day control.
- **Empirical p-values, one-sided:** p ≈ 0.996-0.997 for Phase 6's primary test — a one-sided empirical p-value against the Monte Carlo null distribution (confirmed via the reported values being expressed as "T_obs is worse than X% of the null distribution," consistent with a one-sided test; the scripts' own explicit one-sided/two-sided declaration was not independently re-verified this session).
- **Multiple-testing correction:** the formal framework (`multiple_testing.py`) implements real Bonferroni correction and explicitly refuses (raises `BenjaminiHochbergNotSupportedError`) rather than partially implementing Benjamini-Hochberg. **Not applied to Phase 6/7** — those are backfilled, non-preregistered analyses (Section 18), so no multiple-testing correction was run on them; only ML Experiment 1 went through this framework.
- **Bonferroni alpha:** the specific alpha threshold used by the one formal experiment run (ML Experiment 1) — **UNKNOWN, requires clarification** (not confirmed this session; `experiment_config.py`/`gates.py` were read in full during Phase 4 but the specific numeric alpha wasn't extracted into this file).
- **Train/holdout, chronological splits:** ML Experiment 1 and `train_baseline_model.py`/`save_trained_model.py` both use chronological (not random) splits — confirmed for the baseline model specifically (75/25 chronological, Section 12); ML Experiment 1's exact split ratio not re-confirmed this session, only that it existed and was formally rejected.
- **Minimum sample requirements, seed handling, treatment of ties/missing observations:** **UNKNOWN — requires clarification for the general case.** Confirmed specific instances: `prospective_gex_pipeline.py` excludes rather than substitutes on missing GEX (Section 9); Phase 6 used exactly 2 seeds for reproducibility. No project-wide minimum-sample-size policy was found documented anywhere audited.

---

## 23. P&L accounting map

Trace, per trade, V1's actual accounting (Section 15's citations):

`entry premium (Black-Scholes, hybrid vol)` → **spread adjustment** (`× (1 + tiered_spread%/2)`) → `contracts` (`max(int(starting_capital × 1% / (entry_premium × 100)), 1)`) → `cost = entry_premium × contracts × 100` (deducted from account cash) → **checkpoint valuation** (re-priced every 5-min bar via Black-Scholes at that bar's spot + same-day vol) → `exit premium (Black-Scholes at exit checkpoint)` → **exit spread/cost treatment** (`× (1 − tiered_spread%/2)`) → `proceeds = exit_premium × contracts × 100` (credited to account cash) → **gross P&L** `= proceeds − cost` → **net P&L** = same as gross here, since nothing further is deducted.

**What IS modeled:** the synthetic tiered bid-ask spread estimate (2-12% by premium tier, applied symmetrically half on each side), a hybrid realized-volatility input.

**What is explicitly NOT modeled anywhere in V1:** commissions, slippage beyond the spread estimate itself, market impact, fees, assignment/exercise mechanics, or expiration settlement mechanics (a position simply marks `exit_reason="expired"` and closes at its last computed theoretical value — no separate settlement/assignment logic was found). **Not independently re-verified this session for every V2/combined-correction variant individually** — the V2 corrections replace the *pricing/spread* inputs with real EODHD bid/ask, but none of them were found to add commissions, slippage, fees, or assignment modeling either; treat this as consistent across the whole V1→V2 chain unless a specific script is checked and found otherwise.

---

## 24. Synthetic vs. real historical vs. paper/live execution — do not use "realistic" and "real" interchangeably

**Synthetic (modeled, not observed):** V1's entry/exit premiums (Black-Scholes with a realized-vol proxy, Section 15), V1's spread-cost estimate (a tiered formula, not a quoted market spread), the `_estimated_spread_cost_pct()` helper wherever reused (also inside `portfolio_backtest.py`'s "hybrid volatility" — itself synthetic, self-caveated in its own docstring as "a real, principled ESTIMATE... NOT real market-implied volatility," Section 10).

**Historical observed (real, but still not an executable reconstruction):** EODHD's actual quoted bid, ask, IV, volume, OI, and Greeks for a real, specific, dated contract — used throughout the V2 correction chain. **Important distinction the user explicitly asked to preserve: a backtest using real historical EODHD data is still not proof of what an actual intraday trade would have filled at.** EODHD is EOD-only (Section 1's own terminology note, `v2_iv_pricing_model.py`'s docstring) — it establishes what a *real, quoted, end-of-day* price was, not what a specific 5-minute-bar-triggered signal could have actually executed at during the trading day. The combined correction (-$45,477.00) is the most complete real-execution *estimate* this project has, not a guaranteed reconstruction of actual fills.

**Prospective/live paper:** Alpaca real-time quotes, real (paper) order submission, real fill-price polling (`get_order_by_id()`, 10×1s attempts before falling back to a pre-trade estimate — Section 11). This is the only category where an actual order was actually placed and actually filled (in a paper account) — genuinely executed, not modeled, but not real capital either.

---

## 25. Live/paper system vs. research system — explicit boundary

**RESEARCH (Sections 3, 5, 13, 17-19):** frozen V1, all V2 pricing corrections, the GEX historical/System-1 research pipeline, ML Experiment 1, Feature Factory, Phase 6/7 forensic attribution, the formal gates/verdict framework.

**PAPER (Sections 8, 11, 12, 26):** the stock bot (`main.py`/`live_trader.py`), the options bot (`options_shadow_loop.py`/`live_options_trader.py`/`options_exit_monitor.py`), the SPY gamma bot (`spy_gamma_shadow_loop.py`/`spy_gamma_daytrader.py`/`spy_gamma_execution.py`).

**Does any code/data flow from paper back into historical research? Checked directly, not assumed: no meaningful flow found.** The paper systems write to their own DBs (`trades.db`, `options_trades.db`, `signal_log.db`, `gamma_forward_test.db`, `ml_shadow_predictions.db`) — none of which any research/backtest script was found reading from, during any phase of this session's audit. The one place live and research code genuinely overlap is *shared library code*, not data: `entry_scorer.py`, `backtest_engine.get_historical_bars()`, `options_pricing.py`, and `risk_manager.py` are all imported by both live and backtest files (Sections 8-9, 11) — meaning a change to one of those shared modules would affect both systems simultaneously, but this is code-sharing, not paper-data leaking into the research baseline. **The paper systems have not silently redefined the research baseline** — V1's frozen parameters (Section 15) do not depend on anything the live systems have logged.

## 27. Risk-control disconnect — a research question, not a code change

**Where the controls exist:** `risk_manager.py`'s `check_max_positions()` (cap 3), `check_daily_loss_limit()` (3%), `check_drawdown_pause()` (8%) — pure, side-effect-free functions, imported and enforced in all three live paths above, plus the *stock-only* backtest (`backtest_engine.py`). **Confirmed absent from the options backtest** (`options_backtest_engine.py`/`portfolio_backtest.py`) — zero grep matches for any of the three functions or `calculate_position_size` (Section 11).

**Can V1 violate them? Yes, measured this session (Section 16), without modifying V1 itself:**
- Max-3-simultaneous-positions: almost certainly violated on the large majority of trading days if V1 had been run as one shared account (88/90 days showed >3 concurrent positions by a quick date-level simulation; the exact trade-blocked-count has a known overcounting artifact and needs a bar-level re-simulation for a precise figure — Section 16).
- Daily-loss-limit (3%) and drawdown-pause (8%): **zero triggers**, using a realized-P&L-at-exit approximation of a single shared account's equity curve. This specific pair of findings is clean and trustworthy as computed, with the caveat that it doesn't capture intraday unrealized-P&L swings.

**Does this materially affect comparability between historical V1 and paper V1? Yes — directly, on the max-positions axis.** The paper systems would have refused a large fraction of V1's actual historical entries had they been subject to the same trade flow, meaning the live systems' real trade-count/frequency is structurally different from what V1's backtest assumes was achievable. The daily-loss/drawdown axis, by contrast, appears not to have been a binding constraint historically (though the caveat above means this isn't fully conclusive either).

**This is recorded here as an open research question, per explicit instruction — V1 has NOT been modified and this finding does not change any code.** A candidate follow-up experiment: a precise, bar-level (not date-level) re-simulation of all three risk controls against the true chronological trade sequence, to get an exact trades-blocked count rather than the current directionally-correct-but-imprecise estimate.

---

## 29. Phase 7 interpretation — what's known vs. not yet proven

**KNOWN (directly measured, verified this session):**
- Every stop-loss-exited trade lost money; every reversal-guard-exited trade was profitable (Section 13) — **but this is near-definitional given how the two exit rules are constructed**, not itself evidence of skill.
- Historical P&L is highly concentrated: 97.7% from 5 trades (Section 13).
- Phase 6 found no standalone entry-signal edge (Section 13, 18).
- Correcting only the volatility input (no execution costs touched) flips the sign from positive to solidly negative (Section 13, 17).
- Same-day trades contribute a near-mechanically-guaranteed loss; multi-day trades still lose money on average even after removing that bucket (Section 13).

**NOT YET PROVEN — do not claim these:**
- That option convexity *itself* caused V1's positive synthetic P&L. The exit-attribution finding is consistent with this hypothesis but does not establish it as the mechanism — an alternative explanation (the concentration/outlier-luck finding, Section 13) is equally consistent with the same data.
- That the exit structure would remain profitable out-of-sample.
- That the mechanism (whatever it is) survives realistic historical IV, shared-capital constraints, and execution costs *simultaneously* — each has only been tested somewhat separately so far (Section 13's synthesis combines several, but Phase 7 Analyses 4/8/9 are still pending, Section 26).
- That the top 5 trades are representative of anything repeatable, rather than idiosyncratic outliers.

**Treat "convexity + asymmetric exits" as a hypothesis, not a conclusion, until Analyses 4/8/9 and further out-of-sample/shared-capital/execution-cost validation support it.**

---

## 30. Research decision tree

```
Phase 6: raw signal, no standalone edge found
  |
Phase 7: forensic attribution of the frozen +$20,427.64
  |-- Is it reproducible? YES (confirmed exactly, multiple times, this session)
  |-- Is it broad-based or concentrated? CONCENTRATED (97.7% from 5 trades)
  |-- Can a mechanism be isolated? PARTIALLY (exit-structure attribution done; convexity not yet proven, Section 29)
  |
Does it survive realistic pricing?
  |-- IV-only correction: NO (flips to -$10,072.44)
  |
Does it survive realistic execution (spread + real bid/ask)?
  |-- Combined correction: NO (-$45,477.00; multi-day-only subset still negative, Section 13)
  |
Does it survive shared-capital constraints?
  |-- NOT YET FULLY TESTED (Section 16/27's max-positions finding suggests likely NOT, but only date-level-precision so far)
  |
Does it survive walk-forward / true out-of-sample testing?
  |-- NOT YET DONE
  |
Only THEN consider new features / GEX / ML on top of this mechanism
  |-- Current status: do not add complexity to a mechanism that has not yet been demonstrated to be real
```

The exact structure may be refined as more of Phase 7 completes — this is the current best read of the repository, not a fixed commitment.

A pure price/momentum cross-sectional ranking is. 6 tickers is thin for cross-sectional breadth (typically wants dozens+). | **Partially realistic** — a momentum-based cross-sectional variant is buildable now; a valuation-based one is not, for lack of data, not lack of effort. Universe breadth is the bigger practical constraint. |
| **9. A *coarser* intraday (1-5 min) dynamics study is possible using Alpaca + Massive, but that is a materially different, lesser thing than tick-level microstructure — don't conflate the two if this is ever revisited. |
| **10. XGBoost/nonlinear ML** | The entire ML Experiment 1 pipeline (feature engineering, chronological train/test split, gates/verdict framework) already exists and is reusable — only the model class (currently Ridge, rejected) would need to change. Already a queued DEFINITE-future-work track (Section 6). | Nothing major — this is the smallest lift of the ML-related areas precisely because the surrounding infrastructure is already built and validated. | **High feasibility.** Most of the hard part (feature pipeline, honest chronological split, formal verdict framework) already exists; swapping in a nonlinear model class is comparatively small. |

Already a queued DEFINITE-future-work track (Section 6). | **Everything else** — no sequence-windowing utility, no TensorFlow/Keras/PyTorch anywhere in the codebase (confirmed by grep above), no training/validation pipeline for sequential data. | **Data exists, infrastructure does not** — this is a from-scratch build, not an extension of anything already validated here. Already the lowest-lift queued track (Section 6). | The actual Monte Carlo simulation code (path generation under GBM or another process) doesn't exist yet, but would sit directly on top of already-validated pricing infrastructure. | **High feasibility**, confirmed consistent with Section 6's existing assessment — most of the hard, trust-sensitive part (real IV, validated BS formula) is already done. |
